| E11 | ~~**`VK_EXT_shader_demote_to_helper_invocation`/`shaderDemoteToHelperInvocation`.** SPIR-V's `OpDemoteToHelperInvocation` needs a new `spirv`->`llvm` conversion pattern (mark the invocation inactive for further side effects, matching HLSL `discard`'s existing non-terminating semantics rather than DXIL's `discard`'s current lowering, if one exists -- audit `SPIRVToLLVMPatterns.cpp` first)~~ (closed, with one premise correction: the audit found no existing `spirv`->`llvm` pattern for *either* `OpKill` or `OpDemoteToHelperInvocation` at all -- and MLIR's own upstream SPIR-V dialect had no op at all for `OpDemoteToHelperInvocation`, despite already having its `Capability`/`Extension` enum cases, so `mlir::spirv::deserialize` would have rejected any real module using it. This row therefore added `spirv.DemoteToHelperInvocation` to MLIR itself (a non-terminator, unlike the deprecated `spirv.Kill`) alongside a new `llvm.spv.demote.to.helper.invocation` LLVM intrinsic (mirroring `llvm.spv.discard`'s shape, but kept distinct since this ICD's own import direction needs to preserve the terminating-vs-non-terminating distinction the emit-direction backend collapses when selecting between `OpKill`/`OpDemoteToHelperInvocation`). `SPIRVToLLVMPatterns.cpp`'s new `DemoteToHelperInvocationConversionPattern` converts the op to that intrinsic; `CanonicalizeStage.cpp` raises it into `feme.stage.demote(true)` -- unconditional, matching `llvm.spv.discard`'s own existing raising -- whose reference/SIMD lowering (`StageOpKind::Demote`) already existed. `shaderDemoteToHelperInvocation` now reads `VK_TRUE` from both the aggregate `VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan13Features` struct and a new dedicated `VkPhysicalDeviceShaderDemoteToHelperInvocationFeatures` struct; `getSupportedDeviceExtensions` gained `VK_EXT_shader_demote_to_helper_invocation` itself, the same "CTS enables it by name regardless of `apiVersion`" reason E3/E5/E6/E8/E9/E10 already established. `OpTerminateInvocation` (roadmap E12) remains a separate, unimplemented op. | E12 | ~~**`VK_KHR_shader_terminate_invocation`/`shaderTerminateInvocation`.** SPIR-V's `OpTerminateInvocation` (a true terminator, unlike `OpDemoteToHelperInvocation`) needs its own conversion pattern lowering to an unconditional discard-and-return. **Closes D3's `graphicsfuzz` 72-case regression** (CTS assumes this promoted-to-1.3 extension is real once `apiVersion >= 1.3`)~~ (closed, exactly as this row's own premise anticipated -- with one premise correction the audit found, the same shape E11's own audit found: no `spirv`->`llvm` conversion pattern existed for `OpTerminateInvocation` at all, and MLIR's own upstream SPIR-V dialect had no op for it either, so a new `spirv.TerminateInvocation` op (a true terminator, requiring no capability beyond the existing `Shader` one) had to be added to MLIR itself first. `SPIRVToLLVMPatterns.cpp`'s new `TerminateInvocationConversionPattern` then converts it into exactly the unconditional discard-and-return this row's own premise specified: a call to the same `llvm.spv.discard` intrinsic `OpKill` itself would use (already raised into `feme.stage.discard(true)` by the existing `CanonicalizeStagePass` renaming, unmodified by this milestone), followed by an `llvm.return`. `shaderTerminateInvocation` now reads `VK_TRUE` from both the aggregate `VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan13Features` struct and a new dedicated `VkPhysicalDeviceShaderTerminateInvocationFeatures` struct; `getSupportedDeviceExtensions` gained `VK_KHR_shader_terminate_invocation` itself, the same "CTS enables it by name regardless of `apiVersion`" reason E3/E5/E6/E8/E9/E10/E11 already established. | E13 | ~~**`VK_KHR_zero_initialize_workgroup_memory`/`shaderZeroInitializeWorkgroupMemory`.** `Workgroup`-storage-class SPIR-V globals need a zero-initializer emitted once per dispatch (likely in `feme::cpu::SPIRVResourceLoweringPass` or a small new pass run before it) rather than reading whatever the host's memory allocator happened to leave behind. **Closes D3's `compute.pipeline.zero_initialize_workgroup_memory` 7-case regression**~~ (closed, with a premise correction the audit found, a different shape than E11/E12's own missing-conversion-pattern one: `Workgroup`-storage-class `spirv.GlobalVariable`s had no conversion pattern at all -- MLIR's own upstream `GlobalVariablePattern` supports only `Input`/`Private`/`Output`/`StorageBuffer`/`UniformConstant` -- so `feme::spirv::WorkgroupGlobalVariablePattern` (SPIRVToLLVMPatterns.cpp) had to be added first, converting one to an ordinary `llvm.mlir.global` in address space 3 (the same convention Clang's own HLSL `groupshared` codegen already uses), alongside a new pointer-type conversion routing an access chain through the same address space. Importing the zero-initializer itself needed two further MLIR-level fixes: `spirv.GlobalVariable` gained `zero_initialized`, a new unit attribute representing the one further shape SPIR-V's `Initializer` operand permits (a plain `OpConstantNull`, which has no symbol of its own to reference, unlike the existing `initializer` symbol-reference case), and `processConstantNull` (the deserializer) was generalized to build a null value for a composite (`spirv.array`/`spirv.struct`) type too, not just a scalar/vector/tensor one -- both real, independent gaps, not specific to this row's own feature. `feme::cpu::GroupSharedLayout` gained `NeedsZeroInit` (GroupShared.h), read off the imported global's own `hasInitializer()`; `feme::cpu::EntryWrapperPass` `memset`s the whole flat groupshared buffer to zero, once per group, when it is set (EntryWrapper.cpp) -- deliberately over-broad (zeroing every groupshared global in the module, not just the flagged one) but still correct, since a groupshared global with no zero-initializer of its own was already free to read as anything. `shaderZeroInitializeWorkgroupMemory` now reads `VK_TRUE` from both the aggregate `VkPhysicalDeviceVulkan13Features` struct and a new dedicated `VkPhysicalDeviceZeroInitializeWorkgroupMemoryFeatures` struct; `getSupportedDeviceExtensions` gained `VK_KHR_zero_initialize_workgroup_memory` itself, the same "CTS enables it by name regardless of `apiVersion`" reason E3/E5/E6/E8/E9/E10/E11/E12 already established. A targeted CTS run found this row's own "Closes ... 7-case regression" text undercounts both directions: the real `compute.pipeline.zero_initialize_workgroup_memory` group is 644 cases, of which 4 (`types.{bool,float32_t,int32_t,uint32_t}`, the exact scalar zero-init shape this row's own scope targets) now genuinely `Pass`, while the remainder stay `Fail`/`NotSupported` behind two further pre-existing, out-of-scope gaps this row's own audit found but does not fix: `OpTypeArray`'s Length operand accepting only a normal constant, not the specialization constant every non-scalar case in this CTS source sizes its array from; and a vector/matrix `shared` variable's own element access producing a vector (not scalar) GEP base LLVM's translation rejects. See "Roadmap E13: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full per-subgroup breakdown, including a third, genuine robustness gap (an `i1`-composite GEP crashing `mlir::translateModuleToLLVMIR` outright) found and isolated but left unfixed, same as the other two) | §1.2/§1.6 | `feme/lib/Target/CPU` | P0 |

This milestone's own scope is the descriptor object model only, per its "closes: none": no `feme::cpu::SPIRVResourceLoweringPass` conversion consumes an inline uniform block from a real dispatch yet, the same "object model first, shader consumption later" shape V5's image/sampler descriptor types already established (`FeMeVulkanDesign.md`'s Descriptor Model table updated to match). A targeted CTS run found a genuine, in-scope limits bug this row's own premise did not anticipate: `dEQP-VK.api.info.vulkan1p2_limits_validation.ext_inline_uniform_block` requires `maxPerStageDescriptorUpdateAfterBindInlineUniformBlocks`/`maxDescriptorSetUpdateAfterBindInlineUniformBlocks` to meet the same `>= 4` floor as their non-`UpdateAfterBind` counterparts *unconditionally*, independent of `descriptorBindingInlineUniformBlockUpdateAfterBind`'s own value -- unlike Vulkan 1.2's own descriptor-indexing `UpdateAfterBind` limits, which stay `0` uncontested alongside a `VK_FALSE` `descriptorIndexing`; fixed by reporting both equal to their non-`UpdateAfterBind` counterparts. Of the 136 `*inline_uniform_block*`-named cases run, 3 genuinely `Pass` (the feature/property advertisement itself), 17 fail at graphics pipeline creation (this ICD is compute-only, unrelated to this row), and 8 (`descriptor_copy.compute.inline_uniform_block_*`) fail cleanly at compute pipeline creation -- a real compute shader consuming the binding, exactly the deferred-dispatch-consumption scope this row's own text states. See "Roadmap E14: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown, including a full `dEQP-VK.api.*`/`dEQP-VK.binding_model.*` regression check finding no crash or new failure from advertising this extension) | none | `feme/lib/Vulkan/Descriptor.{h,cpp}` | P1 |

Split off as new row E22 (copy/sampling pipeline rework, flips the feature bit once it lands), analogous to how E15 itself split into E20/E21. A regression build/test run (`ninja check-feme`, RelWithDebInfo + assertions + ccache) caught a real bug during ASTCDecodeTest.cpp authoring, not just confirmed no regression: the initial ISE range-shape lookup only checked bit-only ranges up to 127, one short of the 8-bit (255) range color endpoints commonly use, which would have silently substituted the next-narrower range's values for any color endpoint quantized at 255 -- fixed and covered by a dedicated test case before this row's own code was considered done) | none | `feme/lib/Vulkan/{Format,Image}.{h,cpp}`, new `feme/lib/Vulkan/ASTCDecode.{h,cpp}` | P2 |

Unlike `decodeASTCBlock` (UNORM8 output), this function returns full-precision floats and handles a block's endpoints being LDR, HDR, or a mix across partitions (CEM 14 pairs an HDR RGB with an LDR alpha) through one shared interpolation pipeline, per the specification's own text. `vkCreateImage` still rejects every ASTC `VkFormat` outright and `textureCompressionASTC_HDR` stays `VK_FALSE`, the same E20/E22 boundary this row inherited unchanged -- E22's copy/sampling pipeline rework covers both the LDR and HDR feature bits once it lands. | E22 | ~~**ASTC LDR copy/sampling pipeline wiring**, the scope E20's own closing note split off: rework `ImageOps.cpp`'s copy/blit/resolve paths (and any shader-image-sampling path that reaches one) to address a block-compressed `Image` per block rather than per texel, wire `feme::vulkan::decodeASTCBlock` into whichever of those paths needs decoded texel data, remove `vkCreateImage`'s current outright rejection of an ASTC `VkFormat`, and only then flip `VkPhysicalDeviceFeatures::textureCompressionASTC_LDR` from `VK_FALSE` to `VK_TRUE`~~ (closed, with a scope narrowing this row's own text did not anticipate: `vkCreateImage` no longer rejects a block-compressed `VkFormat` (`Image.cpp`); `Image::blockPointer`, a block-grid-addressed analogue of `texelPointer`, lets `CommandBuffer.cpp`'s `vkCmdCopyImage`/`vkCmdCopyBufferToImage`/`vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer` address one a whole block at a time -- `bytesPerBlock`/`blockWidth`/`blockHeight` (Format.h) replace `formatElementSize` throughout those paths, a no-op change for a non-block-compressed format since the two agree there, so one implementation now covers both families; `ImageOps.cpp`'s `runResolveImage` rejects a block-compressed format outright (never multisampled in real Vulkan, so meaningless input, not a case to silently mishandle); and `runBlitImage` rejects a block-compressed *destination* (no ASTC encoder exists -- this ICD's ASTC support is decode-only) and an HDR ASTC *source* (`decodeASTCBlock` is LDR-only; `decodeASTCBlockHDR`'s float-producing interface doesn't fit the UNORM8 unpack/pack path every other format here shares), but decodes an LDR ASTC source's fetched texels through `feme::vulkan::decodeASTCBlock` before resampling -- this row's only caller of that decoder, since a copy reinterprets whole blocks verbatim and never decodes. `textureCompressionASTC_LDR` now reads `VK_TRUE` (`PhysicalDeviceInfo.cpp`). The scope this row's own premise did not anticipate: a shader's own *sampling* of a block-compressed image is a separate path from `ImageOps.cpp`/`CommandBuffer.cpp`'s copy/blit/resolve operations entirely -- `CommandBuffer.cpp`'s `materializeImageDescriptor` hands the CPU runtime (`feme/runtime/CPU/FeMeRuntimeCPU.c`, a separate C module with no dependency on this C++ decoder) a raw `Image::data()` pointer for its own per-texel `femeRTFetchTexel2D` to read directly, and that runtime's `femeRTImageFormatElementSize` has no block-compressed case (returns 0, so a sample safely reads as all-zero rather than misinterpreting compressed bytes, but does not decode real ASTC data either). Six of E29's seven crashes (E29a-E29f) are closed with real fixes and new
regression tests, each independently verified by running its own full
`dEQP-VK` group to completion afterward (previously crashing partway
through every one of them); the seventh (E29g) did not reproduce after
two full, clean re-runs of its entire group and is closed as an
unreproducible environmental flake instead. See "Roadmap E29: measured
impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full per-group before/after.

Recommended independent lanes for parallel distribution (each lane's rows
share files/reviewers; lanes themselves are independent once E1/E2 land):
**Lane 1** (sync/dispatch): E3, E6. **Lane 2** (memory/image queries): E4,
E5, E16, E18. **Lane 3** (compute/shader): E7, E8, E11, E12, E13 (E12/E13
are P0 and should not wait for E7/E8/E11 in the same lane). **Lane 4**
(descriptors/misc small extensions): E9, E10, E14, E19. **Lane 5**
(shader compilation, no feature-bit dependency, start immediately): E17.
**Lane 6** (new subsystem, largest single item in this section, good
candidate for a dedicated owner rather than a quick task): E20 (closed),
then E22 (closed) and E21 (HDR formats, closed), both having depended on
E20 but independent of each other, then E23 (shader-sampling wiring,
closed, depended on E22), then E24 (closed, pre-existing capability-query
gap E22's own CTS run found, independent of E20-E23's own ASTC-specific
scope), then E25 (closed, broadened real per-format feature support, the
narrower gap E24's own CTS run found, depended on E24), then E26 (closed,
integer-format image sampling, the gap E25's own investigation found,
depended on E25). **Lane 7** (found by a full CTS run, independent of
every lane above): E27, E28 (both closed), then E29a-E29g (all closed,
seven crashes found by the same run -- six real fixes, one unreproducible
flake).

#### 1.9.5 Closing the Vulkan 1.4 mandatory floor (F-series)

Same granularity and testing discipline as §1.9.4. Every row here depends
on E1/E2's struct plumbing (the 1.4 aggregate cases, not just the 1.3
ones) but not on any specific E-row otherwise, so — per §1.9.3's Strategy
B — this whole section can start as soon as E1/E2 land if enough
independent capacity exists, even before §1.9.4 fully closes.

Closed the correctness half of that gap: no FP op conversion pattern ever sets fast-math flags or a non-default rounding mode, so every `spirv.FAdd`/`FMul`/etc. this conversion produces is already the strict, round-to-nearest-even, denormal-preserving code `DenormPreserve`/`RoundingModeRTE`/`SignedZeroInfNanPreserve` ask for at every bit width -- `collectEntryPoints` (`ConvertSPIRVToLLVMPass.cpp`) now reads and accepts those three (still ultimately erased, like every other execution mode, by `ExecutionModePattern`), but rejects `DenormFlushToZero`/`RoundingModeRTZ` with a hard diagnostic instead of silently miscompiling the entry point, since nothing downstream can actually flush denormals or truncate rather than round. The extension itself remains unadvertised (`EntryPoints.cpp`'s properties are all still the conservative "no independent per-width handling" value) since a conformant implementation must support whichever modes it advertises, and `DenormFlushToZero`/`RoundingModeRTZ` still are not -- actually producing flush-to-zero and truncating-rounding code (not just detecting and rejecting shaders that ask for it) is future work; see `agent_thoughts.md`'s F3 entry and F15 below) | §1.2 | `feme/lib/Conversion/SPIRVToLLVM` | P2 |

Both need every arithmetic FP op conversion pattern (`FAdd`/`FSub`/`FMul`/`FDiv`/`FRem`/`FNegate`/converts) to route through LLVM's `llvm.experimental.constrained.*` intrinsics with an explicit rounding-mode/exception-behavior metadata operand and the enclosing function marked `strictfp`, rather than the plain unconstrained ops they emit today, plus a way to represent flush-to-zero (LLVM's `denormal-fp-math`/`denormal-fp-math-f32` function attributes cover `f32` only, not `f16`/`f64` independently, so `float_controls2`'s explicit `FPDenormMode`/`FPRoundingMode` *decorations* on individual instructions -- rather than only a whole-function execution mode -- may need a different lowering strategy entirely). The largest single row F3's own audit produced; consider splitting the constrained-intrinsics plumbing (a prerequisite both the flush-to-zero and rounding-mode-RTZ halves of `VK_KHR_shader_float_controls` share) from the newer `float_controls2` decorations themselves~~ (split as this row's own text suggested: the constrained-intrinsics-plumbing prerequisite closed as F15a -- `RoundingModeRTZ` now genuinely produces truncating-rounding-mode code, verified against LLVM's real, in-tree SPIRV backend, though still not advertised pending an unrelated `feme::cpu` resource-lowering gap a targeted CTS run found -- `DenormFlushToZero`'s own flush-to-zero lowering split off as F15b, and `VK_KHR_shader_float_controls2`'s newer per-instruction decorations split off as F15c; `spirv.FNegate` turned out not to need any of this at all, since negation is an exact sign-bit flip LLVM has no constrained form of; see `agent_thoughts.md`'s F15a entry and F15b/F15c below) | F3 | `feme/lib/Conversion/SPIRVToLLVM` | P2 |

| F12a | ~~**A `std140` uniform buffer array's `spirv.AccessChain` fails SPIR-V->LLVM legalization when dynamically indexed, split off F12's own measured-impact finding.** `dEQP-VK.pipeline.monolithic.push_descriptor.compute.incremental_updates*` (4 cases) all fail `vkCreateComputePipelines` with `failed to legalize operation 'spirv.AccessChain' that was explicitly marked illegal`, indexing a `layout(std140) uniform Input { uint data[16]; the real stride is instead carried as `spirv.VulkanBuffer`'s own new third integer parameter, read back by a new `HandleKind::UniformArray` case in `feme::cpu::SPIRVResourceLoweringPass::classifyVulkanBufferHandle`, which multiplies the dynamic index by it exactly like a storage buffer's own. Recommended independent lanes: **Lane 1** (shader compilation, no
feature-bit dependency once E1/E2 land): F2, F3, F4, F16. **Lane 2**
(rasterizer/graphics pipeline): F5, F6, F13. **Lane 3** (command
buffer/index/memory): F7, F14. **Lane 4** (descriptors, sequence F12 and
E6 together): F12. **Lane 5** (robustness/protected access, sequence
after E16): F9, F10. **Lane 6** (new subsystems, largest single items,
good candidates for a dedicated owner rather than a quick task): F1,
F11.

else in this document, "G0"-"G8" is a graphics milestone.)

#### 1.9.7 Closing the graphics conformance surface (H-series)

§1.9.4 and §1.9.5 close the *core version* floor: the feature bits, limit
fields and promoted extensions a device claiming `VK_API_VERSION_1_4` must
report truthfully. They do not, on their own, make this a graphics device.
[FeMeVulkanDesign.md](FeMeVulkanDesign.md)'s "Conformance Target" now
declares graphics part of the conformance claim rather than a capability
that may or may not be added, so the gaps below stop being "correctly
`NotSupported`" and become tracked rows.

The evidence this section is derived from is
[VulkanCTSReport.md](VulkanCTSReport.md)'s own headline: of the 26 groups
with **zero** failures, `tessellation`, `geometry`, `multiview`,
`mesh_shader`, `wsi`, `transform_feedback`, `fragment_shading_rate`,
`fragment_shader_interlock`, `fragment_shading_barycentric`, `depth` and
`shader_object` are zero precisely because nothing behind them is
advertised. Under the old scope that was the correct end state; under this
one it is the measurement of how much graphics surface is missing.

Same granularity and testing discipline as §1.9.4: one row, one owner, one
commit series, one lit/unittest addition, and one targeted `deqp-vk`
before/after per §1.9.6's G3. "G5"/"G6"/"G7"/"G8" in the Depends-on column
are FeMeGraphicsDesign.md milestones (§1.8.5), not §1.9.6's own G-rows.

| # | Task | Depends on | Files | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|---|

`multiviewGeometryShader`/`multiviewTessellationShader` stay `VK_FALSE` (H4/H5); `VK_KHR_multiview` is added to `getSupportedDeviceExtensions` by name. `unittests/Vulkan/{RenderPass,DrawTest,PhysicalDeviceInfo}Test.cpp` gain layered-framebuffer-acceptance/rejection cases, a real end-to-end two-view/two-layer/two-`gl_ViewIndex`-color draw (`DrawTest.MultiviewRendersDifferentColorPerViewIntoItsOwnLayer`, confirmed correct per-layer output), and the flipped feature-bit expectation; | H2a | ~~**Root-cause the `feme-cpu-simdize` divergent-vector shape `dEQP-VK.multiview`'s own fragment shaders hit** (454 of 838 cases in H2's own measured run): a shape distinct from the four `phi`/`select`/`shufflevector`/`extractelement` patterns roadmap C3 already closed, since C3's own fix does not reach it. Likely a new member of C8's "shader long tail" bucket rather than a new root cause of its own — triage against C8 first~~ (done -- root-caused, not fixed: this row's own framing did not hold. Every one of the 454 cases is the *vertex* shader's `gl_Position` write via glslang's implicit `gl_PerVertex` interface block (`{Position, PointSize, ClipDistance[1], CullDistance[1]}`), not a fragment shader's output. SPIR-V decorates a `BuiltIn` interface block's members individually (`OpMemberDecorate`), not the block variable itself (`OpDecorate`) the way a standalone builtin variable is, but `SPIRVToLLVMPatterns.cpp`'s `buildStageIODecorationsAttr` only ever reads a whole-variable `built_in`/`location` attribute -- so the block's own `llvm.mlir.global` carries no `!spirv.Decorations` metadata at all, `feme::graphics::CanonicalizeStagePass`'s `isSPIRVStageIOGlobal` never recognizes it, and its `gl_Position` store is left exactly as raw as it started, reaching `feme::cpu::SIMDizePass` directly and correctly diagnosing the now-divergent vector value it was never supposed to see. Triage against C8 confirms it is a new member of C8's "shader long tail" bucket, as this row's own text predicted, and a different root cause from both C3 (SIMDize's own decomposition shapes, unaffected -- this gap never reaches SIMDize having been legalized at all) and C8b (a matrix/aggregate value SIMDize cannot widen *once legalized*; this one is never legalized in the first place). See "Roadmap H2a: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full reproduction. Needs a per-member decorations encoding (extending, not replacing, the existing whole-variable `!spirv.Decorations` shape `feme::spirv::attachStageIODecorations`/`parseSPIRVDecorations` already round-trip) attached to the block's global, covering `gl_PerVertex`'s four members at minimum~~ (done -- `StageIOGlobalVariablePattern` now also checks whether a stage-IO variable's pointee is a `mlir::spirv::StructType`, and if so builds a *second* `llvm.mlir.global` attribute, `feme.spirv.member.decorations` (`buildMemberDecorationsAttr`/`buildMemberDecorationTuple`, new in `SPIRVToLLVMPatterns.cpp`), from its members' own `OpMemberDecorate`d `BuiltIn`/`Location`/`Component`/`Index`/interpolation decorations, in an `(memberIndex, tuples)` shape whose `tuples` reuses the existing whole-variable `(i32 decoration, i32 arg...)` tuple encoding unchanged. `feme::spirv::collectStageIOMemberDecorations`/`attachStageIOMemberDecorations` (`StageIODecorations.cpp`, `SPIRVToLLVM.h`) mirror the existing whole-variable collect/attach pair, turning it into `feme.spirv.MemberDecorations` metadata on the real `llvm::GlobalVariable`, and `SPIRVToLLVMTranslator.cpp` wires both into the same collect-before/attach-after `translateModuleToLLVMIR` sequence the whole-variable channel already uses. **Deviation**: unlike `!spirv.Decorations`, this new metadata kind is *not* real SPIR-V backend metadata -- `OpMemberDecorate` decorates a struct *type*, not a global variable, so there is nothing for LLVM's SPIRV backend to attach a per-member decoration to at the global-variable granularity `!spirv.Decorations` uses; `spirv-to-llvm-stage-io.mlir` gains a `gl_PerVertex`-shaped case and a new `spirv-to-llvmir-stage-io-member-decorations.mlir` covers the full end-to-end `!feme.spirv.MemberDecorations` metadata shape. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1810/1811 (1 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`), up from 1804/1805 before this row. Closes the whole `dEQP-VK.multiview` group's own remaining largest blocker~~ (done, with the "closes the whole group" framing narrower than claimed, matching H2's own precedent: `isSPIRVStageIOGlobal` now recognizes a `feme.spirv.MemberDecorations`-only global, and `canonicalizeSPIRVStage` decomposes it into one `SignatureElement` per struct member (`gl_Position` -> `SignatureSystemValue::Position`; `gl_PointSize`/`gl_ClipDistance`/`gl_CullDistance` -> `None`, changed from this row's own dead, never-before-reachable `ClipDistance`/`CullDistance` mapping). **Deviation**: the first landing modeled a builtin interface block's own load/store as one whole aggregate value, a shape a real `deqp-vk` run found does not occur in practice -- real SPIR-V-derived IR addresses each member (even each individual component of `gl_Position`) with its own scalar load/store, either a bare global (SPIR-V's own offset-0 access, which LLVM's constant-`getelementptr` folding erases entirely) or a `getelementptr (i8, ptr @block, i64 ByteOffset)` `ConstantExpr` (LLVM's own canonical byte-offset form, not a struct-member-indexed `getelementptr`), crashing the first landing's `cast<StructType>` outright. | H2e | ~~**A SPIR-V `Output` storage-class variable read back after being written within the same invocation** (`dEQP-VK.multiview.input_instance`'s own shape, 24 of H2d's own measured 421 failures): unlike DXIL's `loadInput`/`storeOutput` split (where an output is genuinely write-only), SPIR-V's `Output` storage class permits reading back a value already written in the same invocation (e.g. a compound `gl_Position.x += 1.0`-shaped update); `feme.stage.input.load`/`.output.store`'s Input-vs-Output dichotomy (and `ValidateStagePass`'s enforcement of it, `'feme.stage.input.load' ... refers to element N with the wrong direction`) has no representation for this. Needs either a per-element local shadow value `canonicalizeSPIRVStage` can thread through (replacing the read-back load with the last stored value directly, since both sides are already known at legalization time) or a relaxation of `ValidateStagePass`'s direction check for this specific pattern~~ (done, via the first of the two options this row's own text offered: `canonicalizeSPIRVStage` routes every `Output`-direction leaf scalar (one per (`ElementID`, `Row`, `Component`), the granularity `loadStageIOValue`/`storeStageIOValue`'s existing recursion already decomposes every access to) through its own shadow `AllocaInst` (`ShadowValueMap`) instead of a wrong-direction `feme.stage.input.load`; every rewritten store also writes through to its own shadow alloca, and `PromoteMemToReg` (once every instruction has been rewritten) converts every shadow alloca to SSA form, resolving each read-back to the dominance-correct reaching store -- inserting a `phi` for a real control-flow join, exactly the SSA construction a compiler's own `mem2reg` pass performs for a local variable, which a linear "last stored value" scan (`ValidateStagePass`'s own relaxation option) could not do correctly in general. **Deviation**: fixing this row's own root cause does not turn `input_instance` green -- all 24 cases now build and run to completion (`grep -c "wrong direction"` against a full `dEQP-VK.multiview` run now returns 0, down from 24), but land in the same `Fail (Fail)` image-comparison bucket roadmap H2g already tracks (334 -> 358) rather than failing earlier at `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`, so the group's own headline totals (78 pass / 421 fail / 339 not-supported) are unchanged from H2d's own baseline. `CanonicalizeStageTest.{OutputReadBackResolvesToStoredValueStraightLine,OutputReadBackResolvesAcrossControlFlow}` (the latter confirming the `phi` case) and a new `spirv-canonicalize-stage-output-readback.ll` lit test cover the fix; `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1817/1818 (1 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`), up from 1814/1815 before this row. See "Roadmap H2e: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H2d | `feme/lib/Transforms/Graphics/CanonicalizeStage.cpp`, `feme/lib/Transforms/Graphics/ValidateStage.cpp` | P2 |
| H2f | ~~**Occlusion query availability reporting appears incorrect under multiview** (`dEQP-VK.multiview.non_precise_queries_with_availability`'s own shape, 18 of H2d's own measured 421 failures, `occlusion availability bit N is 0`): not yet investigated at all -- root cause unknown, could be the query-pool/multiview interaction itself or something unrelated the multiview test happens to exercise first~~ (done -- root cause: per the Vulkan spec, `vkCmdBeginQuery`/`vkCmdEndQuery` for an occlusion query recorded inside a multiview render pass instance implicitly span one query index per set bit of the active subpass's view mask (ordered by ascending bit position), not the single index a non-multiview query uses. `feme::vulkan::QueryPool`/`CommandBuffer.cpp` had no representation for this: `begin`/`markAvailable` always touched exactly one index, and `runDraw`'s per-view loop summed every rendered view's own passed-sample count into one shared counter applied to every currently-active query pool broadcast-style (`accumulateActiveOcclusionSamples`) -- so a two-view query's second (and every further) implicit index was simply never written, staying permanently unavailable, and its first index held the sum across both views rather than either view's own count. `QueryPool::begin`/`markAvailable` now take a `ViewCount` (the popcount of `GraphicsState::Binding.ViewMask` at `vkCmdBeginQuery` time, 1 outside multiview) and operate on `[Query, Query+ViewCount)`; `accumulateActiveOcclusionSamples` is replaced by `accumulateOcclusionSamples(Query, Samples)`, adding to one explicit index; `CommandBuffer.cpp` tracks each active occlusion query as an explicit `ActiveOcclusionQuery{Pool, FirstQuery, ViewCount}` rather than a plain `QueryPool *` set, and `runDraw`'s per-view loop resets its own passed-sample counter per view, routing each view's own count to its own enumerated query index. `DrawTest.MultiviewOcclusionQueryWritesOneQueryIndexPerView` (a two-view `viewMask == 0b11` render pass, a single `vkCmdBeginQuery`/`vkCmdEndQuery` pair at query index 0) locks this down at the unit level, confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code with exactly this row's own symptom. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1818/1819 (1 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`), up from 1817/1818 before this row. A targeted `dEQP-VK.multiview` re-run (838 cases) confirms `grep -c "availability bit"` now returns 0 (down from 18) and the pass count rises to 96/838 (up from H2e's own 78/838), with the remaining 403 failures unchanged from H2d/H2e's own already-tracked buckets (H2g's 358, H2b's 3 + H8's 14 pipeline-creation failures, H8's 28 format-not-supported failures) -- see "Roadmap H2f: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H2d | `feme/lib/Vulkan/QueryPool.h`, `feme/lib/Vulkan/QueryPool.cpp`, `feme/lib/Vulkan/CommandBuffer.cpp` | P2 |
| H2g | ~~**A widespread rendering-correctness gap spanning most of `dEQP-VK.multiview`'s subgroups** (334 of H2d's own measured 421 failures, now 358 once H2e's own 24 `input_instance` cases joined this bucket instead of failing earlier at pipeline creation -- `clear_attachments`, `index`, `secondary_cmd_buffer`, `readback_{implicit,explicit}_clear`, `multisample{,_resolve}`, `masks`, `instanced`, `input_attachments`, `draw_indirect{,_indexed}`, `draw_indexed`, `stencil`, `depth`, `input_instance`, each in both `renderpass2`/`dynamic_rendering` variants): plain image-comparison failures, the first real check of these tests' rendered output at all (every one previously hit H2a's SIMDize crash before ever reaching image comparison). Spot-checking `instanced.no_queries.15` rules out H2e's own read-back gap (a plain, unconditional multi-branch `gl_Position`/`out_color` write) as the shared cause -- root cause otherwise undetermined, needs its own triage pass before assuming a single fix closes all 358~~ (done -- the triage this row called for, plus the two fixes it found: (1) **SPIR-V's clip-space Y already increases downward** (Vulkan's own window-space convention), but `feme::graphics::Executor::projectVertex`'s viewport transform assumes the opposite, Y-up convention -- correct for DXIL/HLSL's own clip space (the only other producer of a `SignatureSystemValue::Position` output), the executor's actual target convention, but silently double-flipping every SPIR-V vertex shader's `gl_Position` instead of normalizing it first. `canonicalizeSPIRVStage` (`CanonicalizeStage.cpp`) now negates a `Position`-system-value store's Y component (`negateSystemValuePositionY`) before it reaches the executor. This alone raised the group from H2f's own 96/838 to 366/838. (2) **`vkCmdClearAttachments` inside a multiview render pass instance only ever cleared array layer 0**, ignoring every other set `viewMask` bit entirely (`clearAttachmentRects` had no per-view replication at all, unlike `runDraw`'s own `sliceAttachmentLayer` loop) -- confirmed against `clear_attachments.no_queries.15`: layers 1-3 kept their pre-clear draw content where the reference expected the clear color. `clearAttachmentRects` now loops over `RenderTargetBinding::ViewMask`'s own set bits (1, outside multiview) and slices each into its own attachment layer first. This raised the group further to 420/838 (50.1%), fixing all of `clear_attachments`/`readback_explicit_clear` and 6 of `readback_implicit_clear`'s 24. The remaining 79 failures split into 34 newly-triaged, independent gaps (16 `input_attachments` -- a totally blank `Fail (Fail)`, new roadmap row H2h; 18 `readback_implicit_clear`, only its multi-subpass view-mask combinations, new roadmap row H2i) and 45 already-tracked pipeline/render-pass-creation failures unchanged from H2d's own breakdown (H2b's 3, H8's 14 + 28). `DrawTest.MultiviewClearAttachmentsClearsEveryViewsOwnLayer` locks down fix (2) at the unit level; fix (1)'s own `CanonicalizeStageTest` coverage is the existing `spirv-canonicalize-stage-interface-block-byte-offset.ll` lit test, updated to expect the negated value, plus `DrawTest.DynamicLineWidthWidensTheLine`'s own SPIR-V-sourced line position updated to match the corrected convention. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1820/1821 (1 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`). See "Roadmap H2g: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H2d | `feme/lib/Transforms/Graphics/CanonicalizeStage.cpp`, `feme/lib/Vulkan/ImageOps.cpp` | P1 |
| H2h | ~~**`dEQP-VK.multiview.input_attachments` renders a totally blank (`Fail (Fail)`, every pixel black/transparent) image in every one of its 16 remaining cases** (H2g's own triage): unlike H2g's other two fixes (a wrong-but-present value), nothing is drawn at all, suggesting either the subpass-input read itself always returns zero/fails silently under multiview, or the pipeline/subpass wiring for a multiview input-attachment subpass never reaches the draw in the first place. Not yet root-caused; needs its own investigation (dump the subpass-input descriptor heap contents and confirm whether the fragment shader's `subpassLoad` executes at all, or short-circuits)~~ (done -- root cause: `RenderTargetBinding` (`RenderPass.h`), the shape both a classic `VkRenderPass`+`VkFramebuffer` and a `vkCmdBeginRendering` instance normalize into before reaching `CommandBuffer.cpp`'s draw path, never captured `SubpassDescription::InputAttachments` at all, only `Colors`/`Depth`/`Stencil`. `buildSubpassInputHeap`'s only attachment-resolution mechanism was `Gfx.ColorAttachmentInputIndices`'s identity-mapping fallback, built solely for `VK_KHR_dynamic_rendering_local_read` (which has no separate classic input-attachment list, and instead maps a `subpassInput`'s `InputAttachmentIndex` directly onto one of the current subpass's own color attachments). That fallback was silently reused for classic render passes too -- wrong whenever a subpass's input attachment is *not* one of its own color attachments, exactly this test's own shape, where a later subpass reads back an *earlier* subpass's color output through a `subpassInput`. That earlier attachment was never present in the later subpass's own color-attachment list, so `subpassLoad` addressed index 0 against the later subpass's own freshly-cleared color attachment instead, reading transparent black and rendering every pixel of every one of the 16 cases blank. Fix: `RenderTargetBinding` gains an `Inputs` field (one resolved `ImageView *` per `InputAttachmentIndex`, `nullptr` for `VK_ATTACHMENT_UNUSED`), populated by `buildRenderTargetBinding` from `SubpassDescription::InputAttachments` resolved against the whole framebuffer rather than just the current subpass's own color attachments. `runDraw` resolves and per-view-slices these into a new `SubpassInputs` list threaded into a new `buildSubpassInputHeap` parameter of the same name, which is authoritative when non-empty (mapping directly onto input-attachment indices) and falls back to the old identity-mapping logic, unchanged, only when empty (every `vkCmdBeginRendering` instance, which has no classic input-attachment list of its own and so always leaves `RenderTargetBinding::Inputs` empty) -- the two paths cannot conflict. `DrawTest.MultiviewInputAttachmentReadsBackAnEarlierSubpassColorOutput` (a two-subpass classic `VkRenderPass`, `viewMask == 0b11` both subpasses: subpass 0 writes attachment 0 solid red per-view; subpass 1 declares attachment 0 -- not one of its own color attachments -- as its input attachment, reads it back with the existing `SubpassLoadFragmentSource` shader, and writes solid green to attachment 1) locks this down at the unit level; confirmed (via `git stash` of just the fix) to fail on the pre-fix code and pass with it restored. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1762/1821 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`), up from 1761/1820 before this row's own new test. **Measured impact**: a real, targeted `dEQP-VK.multiview` run (838 cases) rose from H2g's own 420/838 (50.1%) to 436/838 (52.0%) passed, `Failed` falling from 79 to 63 -- exactly this row's own 16 cases, confirmed individually (`input_attachments`/`renderpass2.input_attachments`, 8/8 and 8/8 now `Pass`), with every other remaining `Failed` case unchanged and independently tracked (roadmap H2i's 18, H8's 42, H2b's 3). `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md`: confirmed no change needed -- this fix corrects existing input-attachment rendering behavior, advertising no new feature or extension. See "Roadmap H2h: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H2g | `feme/lib/Vulkan/{RenderPass.h,CommandBuffer.cpp}` | P2 |
| H2i | ~~**`dEQP-VK.multiview.readback_implicit_clear`'s multi-subpass view-mask combinations still fail** (18 of H2g's own triage, exactly the cases whose numeric suffix names more than one subpass -- e.g. `1_2_4_8`, `5_10_5_10`, `8_1_1_8` -- while every single-subpass combination, e.g. `15`, now passes after H2g's `vkCmdClearAttachments` fix): a per-subpass `VK_ATTACHMENT_LOAD_OP_CLEAR` interacting with multiple multiview subpasses (each its own view mask) inside one render pass instance is not yet correctly modeled -- root cause otherwise undetermined~~ (done -- two compounding bugs: (1) `applyClear` (the `VK_ATTACHMENT_LOAD_OP_CLEAR` handler `CommandBuffer.cpp` runs at `vkCmdBeginRenderPass`/`vkCmdBeginRendering`) never sliced by array layer at all -- unlike H2g's own `clearAttachmentRects` fix, it always cleared only the first attachment layer's own byte range regardless of `RenderTargetBinding::ViewMask`, so even a *single*-subpass case whose mask excluded view 0 (e.g. `8`, view 3 alone) already failed for this reason alone; (2) `vkCmdNextSubpass` never called `applyLoadOps` at all, so a later subpass introducing a *new* view of an attachment already referenced by an earlier subpass (exactly `1_2_4_8`'s own shape: four subpasses, each a disjoint one-bit slice of one shared color attachment's view mask) never got its own share of the load op, leaving that view's own layer with whatever memory already held instead of the clear color. Fix: `applyClear` now slices per view like `clearAttachmentRects` already does, and only clears the views in the current subpass's `ViewMask` a new `GraphicsState::LoadedAttachmentViewMask` map does not yet mark as already loaded for that attachment -- gating against re-clearing a view an earlier subpass already used (which would erase that subpass's own draws); `applyLoadOps` is now called again at every `vkCmdNextSubpass`, not just at the render-pass instance's own start. `DrawTest.MultiviewLoadOpClearAppliesEachSubpassOwnNewlyIntroducedViews` (a three-subpass render pass exercising both fixes: view 0 drawn by subpass 0, view 1's own layer only ever painted by its own load-op share at subpass 1, subpass 2 re-using view 0 without erasing subpass 0's own draw) locks this down at the unit level, confirmed to fail on the pre-fix code and pass with it restored. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1763/1822 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`). A targeted `dEQP-VK.multiview.readback_implicit_clear` run (24 cases) now passes 24/24, up from 6/24; a full `dEQP-VK.multiview` run (838 cases) rises from H2h's own 436/838 (52.0%) to 454/838 (54.2%) passed, `Failed` falling from 63 to 45 -- exactly this row's own 18 cases, with every other remaining `Failed` case unchanged and independently tracked (H8's 42, H2b's 3). See "Roadmap H2i: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H2g | `feme/lib/Vulkan/CommandBuffer.cpp` | P2 |
| H2b | ~~**A pipeline with zero color attachments** (`dEQP-VK.multiview.depth_without_fragment_shader`'s own shape, and presumably the same case outside multiview): `feme::graphics::executeDraws` unconditionally requires at least one (`"a draw needs at least one color attachment"`), but a depth-only pipeline with no fragment shader output is legal Vulkan. Needs the executor's attachment-count validation relaxed to allow zero color attachments when the pipeline itself declares none, not a multiview-specific fix~~ (done, exactly as described, but insufficient to turn the named case green -- see this row's own Deviation: `executeDraws`'s unconditional `Draw.Attachments.empty()` rejection is removed; the existing `Pipeline.getColorBlends().size() != Draw.Attachments.size()` count-match check already enforces the same pipeline/draw consistency in the zero-attachment case as any other, so nothing else in the function needed to change (every `Draw.Attachments` iteration already loops `size()` times, and the extent/dual-source-blend fallbacks already handle an empty color list, per roadmap E5/C4). `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`'s own analogous `Targets->Colors.empty()` rejection (`GraphicsPipeline.cpp`, not named by this row's own text but blocking pipeline creation for the exact same reason, one step earlier) is relaxed the same way; nothing downstream of it (color blend state translation, stage interface validation, the cached attachment-format list) assumes a nonempty `Colors`. `ExecutorTest.RendersWithZeroColorAttachments` (a real depth-only draw, no color attachment bound, a fragment stage with no output at all) and `GraphicsPipelineTest.AcceptsZeroColorAttachments` (a dynamic-rendering pipeline with zero color attachments and a fragment stage writing only `gl_FragDepth`) lock down each fix at the unit level; `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1765/1824 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`), up from 1763/1822 before this row. **Deviation**: a targeted `dEQP-VK.multiview.depth_without_fragment_shader*` run (1 case, plus its `dynamic_rendering`/`renderpass2` siblings a full `dEQP-VK.multiview` run also exercises, 3 total, matching this row's own originally-cited count) still fails identically after both fixes above, `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED` for a *different* reason: `GraphicsPipeline.cpp`'s `if (!VertexInfo || !FragmentInfo) return ... "a graphics pipeline needs both a vertex and a fragment stage"` -- the real CTS case does not merely write no fragment *output*, as this row's own title assumed, it omits the fragment shader stage from `VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo::pStages` entirely, which is separately legal Vulkan (a fragment shader is optional whenever its subpass/dynamic-rendering render target has no color attachments, `VUID-VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo-pStages-06894`/`-06895` and neighbors). Making the fragment stage itself optional is a materially larger change than the attachment-count relaxation this row's own text scoped to -- it touches pipeline creation (`compileAndValidateStages`/`translateStageInterfaces` skipping fragment compilation and interface validation entirely), the compiled-artifact/`GraphicsPipeline` shape (`FragmentStage` becoming genuinely optional rather than always-present), and the executor's whole per-quad fragment-invocation loop (`Executor.cpp`'s `FSSig`/`FSColors`/`FS.invokeFragments` path, currently unconditional), not just its attachment-count check -- so it is left to new row H2j rather than folded into this one. A full targeted `dEQP-VK.multiview` re-run (838 cases) after both of this row's own fixes remains unchanged at 454/838 (54.2%) passed, confirming the fixes are real but do not yet move this row's own named case; see "Roadmap H2b: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full reproduction) | none | `feme/lib/Graphics/Executor.cpp` | P2 |
| H2j | ~~**Make a graphics pipeline's fragment shader stage genuinely optional** (roadmap H2b's own Deviation): `GraphicsPipeline.cpp`'s `translateFixedFunctionState` unconditionally rejects `VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo::pStages` missing a `VK_SHADER_STAGE_FRAGMENT_BIT` entry (`"a graphics pipeline needs both a vertex and a fragment stage"`), but Vulkan permits omitting it entirely whenever the pipeline's render target has no color attachments (depth/stencil-only rendering, `VUID-VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo-pStages-06894`/neighbors) -- exactly `dEQP-VK.multiview.depth_without_fragment_shader`'s own shape (and its `dynamic_rendering`/`renderpass2` siblings), still failing after H2b's own fix for this exact reason. Needs `compileAndValidateStages`/`validateStageInterfaces` to skip fragment-stage compilation and cross-stage interface validation when no fragment stage is named (only when `Targets.Colors.empty()`, matching the VUID's own condition -- a color-attached pipeline still requires one), `GraphicsPipeline`'s `FragmentStage` to become a genuinely optional (`nullptr`) `shared_ptr<CompiledStage>` rather than always-present, and `feme::graphics::executeDraws` to skip its whole fragment-invocation loop (`FSSig`/`Varyings`/`FSColors`/`FS.invokeFragments`) when the pipeline has none, running only vertex-stage clip/rasterize/early-depth-test with no per-fragment shading at all~~ (done: `translateColorBlendState` also needed a matching fix -- see this row's own Deviation -- `translateFixedFunctionState` now only requires a vertex stage, resolving fragment robustness only when a fragment stage is present and only rejecting a missing one once `Targets.Colors` is known non-empty; `compileAndValidateStages` takes `FragmentInfo` as a nullable pointer, skipping fragment compilation and its push-constant/bound-range validation when absent; `validateStageInterfaces` takes `FragmentStage` as a nullable pointer, skipping every fragment-side interface check when null (asserting `FragmentStage || ColorAttachmentCount == 0`); `compileGraphicsPipeline` computes `FragmentModule` and the fragment half of the pipeline cache key conditionally so a fragment-less pipeline still gets a correct, distinct cache key; `GraphicsPipelineArtifact`/`GraphicsPipeline`'s `FragmentStage` is now a genuinely optional (possibly null) `shared_ptr<CompiledStage>` with a new `hasFragmentStage()` query on both the Vulkan-level and executor-facing wrappers; and `executeDraws`'s `FSSig` is now a plain default-constructed `EntrySignature` populated only when `Pipeline.hasFragmentStage()`, leaving it with empty `Elements` to naturally short-circuit every downstream varyings/color-output loop, with a fragment-less pipeline instead taking an early return right after the `Quads.empty()` check that still counts `Draw.PassedSampleCounter` per already-depth-tested passing lane for occlusion queries. **Deviation**: `translateColorBlendState` (not named by this row's own text) still unconditionally validated `Info->attachmentCount == Targets.Colors.size()` even with no fragment stage, rejecting exactly the shape real CTS tests build -- `vktMultiViewRenderTests.cpp`'s `depth_without_fragment_shader` case hardcodes `pColorBlendState.attachmentCount = 1` unconditionally, which is legal Vulkan because a pipeline with no fragment shader has no fragment output interface and `pColorBlendState` must be entirely ignored (`VUID-VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo-renderPass-06055`'s scope only applies when a fragment output interface state is present); fixed by threading a `HasFragmentStage` flag into `translateColorBlendState` that skips its `attachmentCount` check (and the rest of `pColorBlendState`) when false, tightly coupled to this row's own change and fixed as part of it rather than spun off. `GraphicsPipelineTest.AcceptsMissingFragmentStage`, `.RejectsMissingFragmentStageWithColorAttachments`, and `.AcceptsMissingFragmentStageWithMismatchedColorBlendState` (the last exercising the Deviation's own shape) and `ExecutorTest.RendersWithNoFragmentStage` lock down each fix at the unit level; `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1769/1828 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`), up from 1768/1827 before this row. A targeted `dEQP-VK.multiview.depth_without_fragment_shader*` run (3 cases, the exact case this row and H2b both cite) now passes in full, and a full `dEQP-VK.multiview` re-run (838 cases) moves from H2b's own 454/838 (54.2%) baseline to 457/838 (54.5%) -- exactly the 3 named cases, no regressions elsewhere; see "Roadmap H2j: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full reproduction) | H2b | `feme/lib/Vulkan/GraphicsPipeline.cpp`, `feme/lib/Graphics/Executor.cpp`, `feme/include/feme/Graphics/Pipeline.h` | P2 |
| H3 | ~~**Multiple viewports and scissors.** `maxViewports` is hard-coded to 1 (`PhysicalDeviceInfo.cpp:247`) and `vkCmdSetViewportWithCount` and the pipeline path both reject anything else (`GraphicsPipeline.cpp:825`). Needs a per-primitive viewport index in the executor, `ViewportIndex` as a stage output, and `shaderOutputViewportIndex`/`shaderOutputLayer` (both `VK_FALSE` in the aggregate 1.2 struct today) raised in lockstep with H2~~ (done: `PhysicalDeviceInfo.h`'s `MaxViewportCount` (16) replaces the hard-coded `maxViewports == 1`, and `multiViewport` is now advertised `VK_TRUE` too (found missing during this row's own CTS run -- `deqp-vk` gates the whole `shader_viewport_index`/`multi_viewport` CTS surface on this one feature bit, not `maxViewports`' numeric value alone); `GraphicsPipeline.cpp`'s `translateViewportState`/`GraphicsPipelineState`/`DynamicGraphicsState` carry a full `Viewports`/`Scissors` array (`SmallVector<T, MaxViewportCount>`) rather than one element, validating any static count from 1 to 16; `CommandBuffer.cpp`'s `vkCmdSetViewport`/`vkCmdSetScissor` write a `first*`-relative sub-range of the active array, and the new `vkCmdSetViewportWithCount`/`vkCmdSetScissorWithCount` (plus their already-implemented `*EXT` aliases) replace the whole array from element 0, truncating it via a new `RecordedCommand::ResetViewportOrScissorCount` flag rather than only ever growing it; `Executor.cpp`'s `resolvePrimitiveState` reads a vertex stage's `ViewportArrayIndex` output (`gl_ViewportIndex`, SPIR-V `BuiltIn` 10) per primitive and routes each triangle/line/point through the array element (and paired scissor) it names, via a new `resolveViewportArrayIndex` (`LayeredRendering.h`, alongside `resolveRenderTargetArrayLayer`) that discards a primitive whose index is out of range rather than clamping it -- a vertex stage that writes no `ViewportArrayIndex` still resolves to element 0, preserving single-viewport behavior untouched. Along the way, fixed two bugs the previous agent's stashed partial progress had introduced: an ambiguous-overload compile error from `vkCmdSetViewport{,WithCount}{,EXT}`/`vkCmdSetScissor{,WithCount}{,EXT}` calling each other unqualified (fixed by factoring shared logic into private `setViewportImpl`/`setScissorImpl` helpers, never one entry point calling another), and a multiview regression (3 `DrawTest` cases) from `runDraw`'s per-view loop no longer pre-slicing `ResolveAttachments`/`DepthStencil` per view while still pre-slicing color `Attachments`, disagreeing with `Executor.cpp`'s `getDrawLayerCount()` and silently discarding every primitive past view 0 (fixed by restoring the per-view slicing for all attachment kinds, and changing the executor's default `RequestedLayer` from `Draw.ViewIndex` to 0 since attachments arrive already sliced to the current view). `GraphicsPipelineTest.AcceptsMultipleViewportsAndScissors`/`.RejectsTooManyViewports`, `DrawTest.DynamicViewportWithCountRoutesInstancesToDifferentViewports`/`.OutOfRangeViewportIndexDiscardsThePrimitive`, and `PhysicalDeviceInfoTest.ShaderViewportIndexLayerFeaturesAreTrue` lock down each new behavior at the unit level; `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1776/1835 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`). A targeted `dEQP-VK.draw.*.shader_viewport_index.*` run (196 cases) moves from 0/196 passed (100% `NotSupported`, gated on the now-fixed `multiViewport` bit alone) to 64/196 passed, 68 `Failed`, 64 `NotSupported` (the `geometry_shader`/`tessellation_*` variants, unrelated to this row, H4/H5); every `vertex_shader_N` case (`gl_ViewportIndex` written by, and only ever consumed downstream of, the vertex stage) now passes 64/64, while every `fragment_shader_N` case (`gl_ViewportIndex` read back as a fragment-shader input, not just written by the vertex stage) still fails -- broken out as new row H3a rather than fixed here, since it is a separate fragment-stage-signature gap, not a viewport-count/pipeline/executor one. A full `dEQP-VK.multiview` regression re-run (838 cases, since this row's fixes touch the same per-view attachment-slicing/per-primitive layer-resolution code multiview depends on) confirms no regression: 457/838 (54.5%) passed, unchanged from H2j's own baseline. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` updated: `multiViewport` (1.0) and `shaderOutputViewportIndex`/`shaderOutputLayer` (1.2) move to `VK_TRUE`, and `VK_EXT_shader_viewport_index_layer` moves from "Planned" to "Implemented (core, not advertised by name)". See "Roadmap H3: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full reproduction) | H2 | `feme/lib/Vulkan/{PhysicalDeviceInfo,GraphicsPipeline,CommandBuffer}.cpp`, `feme/lib/Graphics/Executor.cpp` | P1 |
| H3a | ~~**`gl_ViewportIndex` read back as a fragment-shader input fails pipeline creation** (roadmap H3's own Deviation, `dEQP-VK.draw.*.shader_viewport_index.fragment_shader_*`, 68 of H3's own measured 196 cases, `GL_ARB_shader_viewport_layer_array`'s other half: `out_color = color[gl_ViewportIndex]`): `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` fails with `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED`, `FragmentWrapper.cpp`'s `lowerFragmentStageOps` reporting "fragment stage wrapper requires attached feme.signature metadata" -- i.e. the fragment entry function reaches code generation with no `feme.signature` metadata attached at all, not merely a mismatched one. `CanonicalizeStage.cpp`'s builtin-to-`SignatureSystemValue` mapping (`getSystemValueForBuiltIn`) already maps SPIR-V `BuiltIn` 10 (`ViewportIndex`) to `SignatureSystemValue::ViewportArrayIndex` regardless of storage class, so the reflection-side mapping itself looks direction-agnostic; root cause not yet isolated to a single line, needs its own investigation into why the fragment stage's globals loop (`CanonicalizeStage.cpp`'s `InputGlobals`/`OutputGlobals` collection) does not see this variable at all for a fragment-shader `Input`-storage-class `ViewportIndex` builtin, unlike the small set of fragment-input builtins already wired up before this milestone (`FragCoord`/`Position`, `FrontFacing`, `SampleId`, `SampleMask`)~~ (done: `CanonicalizeStage.cpp`'s globals loop was never the culprit -- root cause turned out to be four independent, previously-undiscovered gaps entirely downstream of it, only reachable once a real fragment shader used a *bound resource* (e.g. the failing CTS shader's `Colors` UBO), which is why H3's own simpler vertex-side testing never tripped them: (1) `SPIRVResourceLowering.cpp`'s (and the DXIL-oriented `ResourceLowering.cpp`'s) `addResourceEnvParams` rebuilds the stage entry function via `Function::Create`+`copyAttributesFrom` to append resource-heap ABI parameters, but `GlobalObject::copyAttributesFrom` never copies function-attached metadata, silently dropping the `!feme.signature` `CanonicalizeStagePass` had already attached -- fixed by adding an explicit `NewF->copyMetadata(&F, 0)` in both files; (2) `FragmentWrapper.cpp`'s `loadFragmentSystemValue` had no `case SignatureSystemValue::ViewportArrayIndex`, hitting the "unsupported fragment system value" error path -- fixed by adding a new per-lane `ViewportIndex` field to `FemeFragmentInvocation` (`RuntimeABI.h`) and `FragmentInvocationField` (`StageArgsLayout.h`), and the matching read case; (3) the CPU runtime (`FeMeRuntimeCPU.c`) only defined scalar `feme.cpu.resource.load/store.raw.i32`/`.f32`, even though `ResourceCalls.cpp`'s mangling already generically supports vector element types, so the real shader's whole-`vec4` UBO load hit a late JIT "symbol not found" -- fixed by adding `feme.cpu.resource.load/store.raw.v4f32`; (4) `Executor.cpp`'s `resolvePrimitiveState` already computed `gl_ViewportIndex` via `resolveViewportArrayIndex` but discarded it after selecting the viewport/scissor array slot, never threading it into the per-lane `FemeFragmentInvocation` the fragment body reads from -- fixed by adding `ViewportIndex` to `PrimitiveState`/`ScreenTriangle` and filling `Inv.ViewportIndex[Lane]`. New unit tests: `SPIRVResourceLoweringTest.PreservesFunctionMetadataAcrossEnvParamRewrite`, `ResourceLoweringTest.PreservesFunctionMetadataAcrossEnvParamRewrite`, `FragmentWrapperTest.LowersViewportArrayIndexSystemValueInput`, `RuntimeCPUTest.RawLoad/StoreV4F32*` (5 cases), `DrawTest.FragmentShaderReadsBackViewportIndex`; `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1785/1844 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`, +9 over H3's own 1776/1835 baseline -- exactly the new tests above). The real `dEQP-VK.draw.*.shader_viewport_index.fragment_shader_*` CTS surface moves from 0/68 to 68/68 passed; the full `shader_viewport_index` group (196 cases) now measures 132/196 passed (68 fragment + 64 vertex), 64 `NotSupported` (tessellation, H4/H5, unrelated), 0 `Failed`. Lifting that filter is necessary but nowhere near sufficient: FeMe's hull ABI is D3D-shaped and needs **two** separately compiled entry points -- a control-point phase for `HullWrapperPass` and a patch-constant phase for `PatchConstantWrapperPass`, discriminated by `feme::cpu::isPatchConstantPhase`'s `SignatureDirection::PatchOutput` test -- whereas GLSL/SPIR-V compiles a tessellation-control shader to a **single** entry point that writes both its per-vertex outputs and `gl_TessLevelOuter`/`gl_TessLevelInner`, typically with an intervening `OpControlBarrier`. Splitting that one entry into the two FeMe phases is exactly R34's own still-deferred "generalize `EntryWrapperPass`'s barrier-region splitting to the control-point batch ABI" item, and is the real work here. Also needs SPIR-V's tessellation execution modes (`Triangles`/`Quads`/`Isolines`, `SpacingEqual`/`SpacingFractionalEven`/`SpacingFractionalOdd`, `VertexOrderCw`/`VertexOrderCcw`, `PointMode`, `OutputVertices`) mapped onto `feme::graphics::TessellationState`, and `BuiltIn` `TessLevelOuter`/`TessLevelInner`/`TessCoord`/`PatchVertices`/`InvocationId` mapped onto the corresponding `SignatureSystemValue`s. This did **not** need `feme/lib/Transforms/CPU/HullPhase.cpp` (no such file exists; `isPatchConstantPhase` lives in `HullWrapper.h`) or changes to `EntryWrapper.cpp` -- see above. Full assembly of `TessellationState` from both compiled stages plus `VkPipelineTessellationStateCreateInfo::patchControlPoints`, and everything else needed to actually compile and run a tessellated pipeline through Vulkan, remains H4b's job; `dEQP-VK.tessellation.*` is therefore still 0 `Pass`/0 `Fail`/1114 `NotSupported`, unchanged and expected~~ | G5, R34 | `feme/lib/Transforms/Graphics/CanonicalizeStage.cpp`, `feme/lib/Transforms/CPU/{EntryWrapper,HullWrapper,PatchConstantWrapper,HullPhase}.cpp` | P1 |

| H4c | ~~**`splitTessellationControlEntry` (H4a) explicitly rejects a tessellation-control shader whose patch-constant phase reads back an SSA value computed before the barrier** (24 of H4b's own measured 227 `dEQP-VK.tessellation.*` `Fail`s, diagnosed rather than mis-compiled: `"tessellation-control SPIR-V entry point's patch-constant region cannot yet capture SSA values defined before the barrier"`), yet this is the common, real GLSL-compiled shape -- computing a per-patch tessellation factor from data derived from the control-point body (e.g. control-point output positions) and referencing it after `OpControlBarrier`. Needs the split to either re-materialize/clone the relevant pre-barrier computation into the patch-constant phase (correct only when that computation reads no other invocation's per-vertex output, i.e. is not itself a cross-invocation reduction) or thread the captured value through a synthetic patch-scoped storage location both phases can address (likely the right shape in general, since it mirrors how the control-point phase's own per-vertex outputs already cross the barrier through patch-shared storage)~~ (done: implemented the storage-threading design this row itself named as "likely the right shape in general" -- `splitTessellationControlEntry` now creates one new address-space-8 (`Output`-storage-class) `GlobalVariable` per captured pre-barrier SSA value, with a synthetic, collision-free `Location` decoration (`computeNextSyntheticLocation`); stores the value into it right after its own definition in the control-point phase; and loads it back at the top of a new `patchconst.captures` block prepended to the patch-constant phase. No new linking code was needed -- `classifySPIRVElement` already treats such a global as an ordinary per-vertex `Output` in the control-point phase and as an ordinary, non-`FromInputPatch` `Input` in the patch-constant phase (the same "OutputPatch" shape a real `gl_out[]` read-back already uses), so `PatchPipeline.cpp`'s existing `Location`-based `HullToPatchConstant` link picks up the new pair automatically. Sound unconditionally, not just for the common case: SPIR-V only defines cross-invocation reads after a barrier, so anything captured from before the one barrier this pass splits at can only be the current invocation's own state. Locked down by `CanonicalizeStageTest.SplitsHullEntryThreadingCapturedSSAValue`; `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1822/1881 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`). **Measured impact**: this row's own 24 named cases no longer hit the SSA-capture diagnostic and the split now genuinely succeeds, but the group's headline `dEQP-VK.tessellation.*` totals (8 `Pass`/227 `Fail`/879 `NotSupported`) and the `dEQP-VK.draw.*` regression sample (12/139/1806) are byte-identical to H4b's own baseline -- every one of the 24 now reaches real code generation but immediately hits a different, pre-existing C8-bucket legalization gap one step later (`MaskIntrinsics.cpp`'s masked-load/store scalar mangling has no case for a matrix/aggregate element type), so the headline cannot move until that gap closes. That gap now surfaces as a process-aborting `llvm_unreachable` rather than a graceful `Fail` for these 24 cases specifically -- confirmed pre-existing, not introduced by this row (A/B rebuild shows the pre-H4c build never reaches it, stopping at the old diagnostic first) -- broken out as follow-up H4e below rather than fixed in this row, since hardening it is CPU masked-memory-op legalization (C8's own scope), not tessellation-control splitting. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed unchanged (no feature bit or extension touched). | H4d | ~~**A JIT-time `"Symbols not found: [ spirv_var_N, spirv_var_N ]"` error rejects some tessellation-control shaders that do *not* hit H4c's SSA-capture diagnostic** (24 of H4b's own measured 227 `dEQP-VK.tessellation.*` `Fail`s, e.g. all of `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*`): the control-point and patch-constant phases each get their own `CompiledStage::create`/`LLJIT` (H4b's own design, matching every other stage), so this is not a cross-phase symbol collision; more likely `splitTessellationControlEntry`'s clone of the module for one phase leaves a global variable referenced-but-undefined after some later pass (a `GlobalDCE`-shaped pass reachable only from the phase's own new entry point, not the original one) strips its definition while a reference to it survives. Root cause not yet isolated -- needs its own investigation, likely starting from a minimal reproduction of `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.default_domain.glsl_quads_ccw`'s own tessellation-control module reduced through the same `feme-convert-spirv-to-llvm`/`feme-canonicalize-stage` pipeline H4b's own tests used to hand-verify shapes~~ (done: the roadmap's own `GlobalDCE`/two-`LLJIT` hypothesis was a red herring, confirmed by a synthetic no-barrier repro that hit a clean, different error instead of the crash. The real root cause, isolated with a real, glslang-compiled `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.default_domain.glsl_quads_ccw` tessellation-control module fed straight into `vkCreateShaderModule`: `resolveStageIOAccess` (`CanonicalizeStage.cpp`) treated *every* single-`ElementID` stage-IO global (i.e. every one not part of a builtin interface block/struct) as whole-value-only, unconditionally returning `std::nullopt` for any nonzero byte offset -- correct for a scalar global, but wrong for one whose own type is a multi-row array/vector, exactly `gl_TessLevelInner`/`gl_TessLevelOuter`'s own `[2 x f32]`/`[4 x f32]` `BuiltIn`+`Patch`-decorated shape, which every GLSL tessellation-control shader writes one element at a time. Only element 0 (byte offset 0) of such a global ever got rewritten into a `feme.stage.output.store`; every other element's store was left as a raw, unrewritten reference to the never-defined (`external`-only) SPIR-V-imported global -- an unresolvable symbol at JIT link time, exactly `LLJIT`'s own "Symbols not found" error, confirmed reproduced byte-for-byte (`JIT session error: Symbols not found: [ gl_TessLevelOuter, gl_TessLevelInner ]`) before this fix and gone after it. Fixed by applying the same `resolveRowComponent` byte-offset-to-(Row,Component) resolution the builtin-interface-block-member branch already used, to the single-`ElementID` branch's own global type instead of a struct member's. Locked down by `CanonicalizeStageTest.RewritesSPIRVArrayOutputStorePerElementByteOffset` (LLVM-IR level: a bare `[4 x float]` `Output` global written at 4 different byte offsets, all 4 now resolve) and `GraphicsPipelineTest.AcceptsTessellationControlMultiElementArrayOutput` (end to end: a real tessellation pipeline writing 2 elements of a `gl_TessLevelOuter`-shaped `BuiltIn`+`patch` global across a barrier split now JIT-links and creates successfully, confirmed to fail exactly as before this fix when A/B-tested with it reverted); `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1824/1883 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`). **Measured impact**: a real `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*` (glsl-only, 24 cases) run confirms 0 `"Symbols not found"` JIT crashes (was 24/24), but all 24 still `Fail` -- immediately hitting a new, distinct, previously-unreached gap this fix itself surfaces (not introduced by it): `splitTessellationControlEntry` only clones a `.patchconstant` phase when it finds a barrier, but winding's own trivial `layout(vertices=1)` shader (a single output control point needs no cross-invocation sync) has none, so no `.patchconstant` function is ever created and `compileAndValidateStages` -- which always expects one -- now fails cleanly instead (`"no hull entry point named 'main.patchconstant'"`, `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED`, no crash). Broken out as follow-up H4f below rather than fixed in this row, since it is a distinct gap in the barrier-split design (R34/H4a's own scope), not a `resolveStageIOAccess` addressing bug. The same fix, applied at the `resolveStageIOAccess` level rather than winding-specifically, also unblocks 8 more `dEQP-VK.tessellation.shader_input_output.*` cases from a pre-existing, differently-shaped `'llvm.getelementptr' op operand #0 must be LLVM pointer type ...` `Fail` into reaching real code generation -- where they now hit H4e's own already-tracked `MaskIntrinsics.cpp` abort instead (confirmed by A/B rebuild: reverting this fix reproduces the old `getelementptr`-error `Fail` for all 8, unchanged from before). Net result on the full group: **byte-identical to H4b/H4c's own baseline**, 8 `Pass`/227 `Fail`/879 `NotSupported` (32 cases now reach the H4e crash bucket instead of their own prior distinct `Fail` reasons, but `Fail` is `Fail` either way) -- expected, not a sign the fix did nothing, since converting a crash or a wrong-shaped error into a different clean `Fail`/crash does not move any of the three buckets by itself. The `dEQP-VK.draw.*` regression sample (1957 of the 29419-case mustpass list, every 15th case with `*viewport_height*` removed) is also byte-identical to H4b/H4c's own 12 `Pass`/139 `Fail`/1806 `NotSupported` -- 0 regressions, 0 new passes, as expected for a fix scoped to stage-IO array addressing rather than anything reachable from a non-tessellation pipeline. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed no change needed (no feature bit or extension touched). See "Roadmap H4d: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H4b | `feme/lib/Transforms/Graphics/CanonicalizeStage.cpp` | P1 |
| H4e | ~~**`MaskIntrinsics.cpp`'s `appendScalarMangling` calls `llvm_unreachable("unsupported feme.cpu.masked.* element type")` for any masked-load/store element type it does not recognize (matrix/aggregate shapes among them), aborting the whole process instead of diagnosing gracefully** -- newly reached (not introduced) by H4c's own fix: all 24 of H4c's named cases now split successfully and reach `feme::cpu::SIMDizePass`'s masked-memory-op path, which is very likely the same C8-bucket matrix/aggregate-legalization gap H4b's own triage table already names for the 88 `feme-cpu-simdize: ... divergent value ... of vector type` and 74 `llvm.getelementptr` cases (not yet independently confirmed), just reached through a third path. Needs both the root legalization fix (C8's own scope) and, independently and more urgently, hardening this specific function to `emitError`+return a null/sentinel rather than `llvm_unreachable`, so an unsupported shape becomes a graceful per-case `Fail` the way every other gap in this codebase already does, rather than a `SIGABRT` that kills an entire CTS run's remaining cases~~ (done: implemented the more urgent, independent half of this row's own description -- `appendScalarMangling` now returns `bool`, reporting an unsupported element `Type` through its own `LLVMContext::emitError` and returning `false` instead of asserting unreachable; `mangleMaskedMemOpName` now returns `std::optional<std::string>` to propagate that failure; `getOrInsertMaskedLoad`/`getOrInsertMaskedStore`/`getOrInsertMaskedAtomicRMW` and their `createMasked*` wrappers all propagate a `nullptr` in turn; and `feme::cpu::LinearizePass`'s `applyStageMasks` (the only caller of the three `createMasked*` entry points) now checks each for `nullptr`, leaving the original `load`/`store`/`atomicrmw` un-rewritten rather than dereferencing a null `CallInst *`. No new plumbing was needed to turn the resulting `emitError` into a graceful pipeline failure -- `feme::cpu::runPipeline`'s existing `ErrorDiagnosticGuard` already catches it, the same mechanism every other diagnosed-rather-than-asserted gap in this codebase relies on. The root legalization fix (actually widening a matrix/aggregate-typed masked memory access) remains C8's own scope and is not attempted here -- confirmed by a real reproduction: the 24 `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*hlsl*` cases this gap was first found through, and the 8 `dEQP-VK.tessellation.shader_input_output.*` cases H4d's own fix separately routed into it, all still `Fail` (32/32), just cleanly -- a single, uninterrupted `deqp-vk` run now reaches `DONE!` and prints a normal totals summary instead of `SIGABRT`ing partway through, making H4c's own per-case-resume methodology unnecessary for every case that used to hit this abort. Locked down by three new `MaskIntrinsicsTest` cases (one per `createMasked*` entry point, each asserting a `nullptr` result and a captured `DS_Error` diagnostic for a struct-typed operand standing in for an unsupported matrix/aggregate shape) and one new `LinearizeTest` case (a `store {float, float}` under a divergent `feme.stage.discard` mask that must diagnose and survive as a plain, unmasked `StoreInst` rather than crash `LinearizePass`); `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1828/1887 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`). **Measured impact**: the full `dEQP-VK.tessellation.*` group (8 `Pass`/227 `Fail`/879 `NotSupported`) and the `dEQP-VK.draw.*` regression sample (12/139/1806) are both byte-identical to H4b/H4c/H4d's own baselines -- expected, since converting a crash into a clean `Fail` for the same 32 cases cannot move any of the three headline buckets by itself, and no non-tessellation pipeline in this codebase's own test corpus ever creates a masked memory access over a matrix/aggregate-typed value in the first place. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed unchanged (no feature bit or extension touched). The whole entry, in this no-barrier shape, is semantically already "the patch-constant phase" for `OutputVertices == 1` (there is only one control point, so nothing meaningfully distinguishes "per control point" from "per patch" here) -- needs either (a) treating a no-barrier entry as *solely* a patch-constant phase whenever every one of its stage-IO writes is patch-frequency (`Patch`-decorated or a tess-factor `BuiltIn`), with an empty/trivial control-point phase synthesized instead, or (b) the more general fix of cloning the *whole* function as `<entry>.patchconstant` unconditionally when there is no barrier (sound only when `OutputVertices == 1`, since with more than one output control point and no barrier there is by definition no legal way for one invocation to see another's data, so nothing but the current invocation's own patch-frequency writes could safely be duplicated this way either -- needs care not to naively assume general soundness)~~ (done, via option (a) of this row's own two proposed fixes: added `isPatchConstantOnlyEntry(Function &F)` (`CanonicalizeStage.cpp`), which scans every address-space-8 store an entry makes and returns true only when at least one exists and every one is patch-frequency (`Patch`-decorated or a tess-factor `BuiltIn`, matching this row's own condition; conservatively `false` for any global carrying `feme.spirv.MemberDecorations`, since a real GLSL tess-factor write is always a plain global, never an interface-block member), and `splitBarrierlessTessellationControlEntry`, which `CloneFunctionInto`s the whole entry as `<name>.patchconstant` when that holds and replaces the original with a trivial `ret void` stub, leaving `splitTessellationControlEntry`'s old barrier-found behavior untouched otherwise. Option (b) (an unconditional whole-function clone) was rejected after tracing `classifySPIRVElement`'s `HullPatchConstant`-phase branch: it classifies any non-`Patch`-decorated address-space-8 write as `Direction::Input` (a read-back of a captured cross-barrier value, never a store), so cloning a function with an ordinary per-vertex output store into the patch-constant phase would misclassify that store's direction and trip `ValidateStagePass`'s `OutputStore` direction check -- a real correctness bug, not merely a theoretical one; `CanonicalizeStageTest.NoBarrierPatchConstantOnlyEntryIsSplitWhole` covers the new split (trivial control-point stub plus a patch-constant clone with the right `PatchOutput`/`TessFactorEdge` signature element), and the pre-existing `HullStageWithNoBarrierIsNotSplit` (an ordinary per-vertex write, no barrier) continues to correctly not split, confirming the `isPatchConstantOnlyEntry` gate is not over-broad. **Deviation**: fixing this row's own gap immediately exposed a second, distinct blocker -- "compiled stage carries no signature reflection" -- spun off and fixed as roadmap H4g below, since it is not part of this row's own described scope (the split logic itself), and a third, still-open blocker spun off as roadmap H4h. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1829/1888 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`), up from 1828/1887 before this row.
