| H4h | ~~**Fixing H4f and H4g still does not turn any of `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*`'s glsl cases green -- all 24 now fail at a third, later blocker: `validateStageInterfaces` (`GraphicsPipeline.cpp`) unconditionally requires the *vertex* stage to write a 4-component `SV_Position`/`gl_Position` output** (`findSystemValue(*VSSig, SignatureDirection::Output, SignatureSystemValue::Position)`), which is correct for the ordinary vertex -> fragment pipeline (the rasterizer needs a clip-space position from *somewhere*, and without a domain stage the vertex stage is the only producer) but is not a real requirement once a tessellation evaluation stage exists: the CTS's own `winding` test's vertex shader is deliberately and legally empty (`void main (void) {}`; confirmed by reading `vktTessellationWindingTests.cpp` directly), because its tessellation evaluation shader computes `gl_Position` purely from `gl_TessCoord` (`gl_Position = vec4(gl_TessCoord.xy*2.0 - 1.0, 0.0, 1.0);`) and never reads back any vertex-stage output via `gl_in[]`. `validateStageInterfaces` needs to skip (or relax) its `SV_Position` requirement on the vertex stage specifically when a tessellation evaluation stage is present in the pipeline, since in that shape the *domain* stage's own output is what gets rasterized (per `PatchPipeline.cpp`'s `runPatchPipeline`/`Executor.cpp`), not the vertex stage's -- root-caused and isolated as part of H4f/H4g's own re-measurement but deliberately not fixed here, since it is a distinct validation-layer gap, not a stage-splitting or signature-serialization one. | H4i | ~~**With H4h's own `SV_Position` relaxation in place, all 24 `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*` glsl cases now reach real rendering and fail at a fourth, later blocker: a systematic front-face/winding-orientation mismatch in the rasterized image**, not a pipeline-creation error. A real `deqp-vk` run (`dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*glsl*`, 24 cases) shows a consistent pattern across every `_ccw`/`_cw` pair: where the CTS expects a full-viewport quad/triangle of one color, the renderer instead produces the *complementary* culled/uncultured result (e.g. `glsl_quads_ccw` gets "Note: got 4081 white and 15 red pixels" / "Failure: expected only white pixels (full-viewport quad)" while its `_cw` sibling gets "got 0 white and 4096 red pixels" for the same expectation, and the `glsl_triangles_*` cases fail with "triangle orientation is incorrect" at roughly a 50/50 pixel split) -- the shape of a front-face or vertex-winding-order inversion specific to tessellated primitives (the domain stage's own emitted vertex order per `gl_TessCoord`/`VertexOrderCcw`/`VertexOrderCw`, or how the executor's rasterizer classifies a tessellated triangle's front face, likely in `PatchPipeline.cpp`/`Executor.cpp`), rather than a per-fragment color or depth bug (the two colors present in every failing image are always exactly the two the test itself defines for "correctly wound" vs "incorrectly wound"). Root cause not yet isolated -- needs its own investigation, likely starting from `vktTessellationWindingTests.cpp`'s own pass/fail image classification alongside `PatchPipeline.cpp`'s tessellator-output vertex ordering for each of `VertexOrderCcw`/`VertexOrderCw` and `Triangles`/`Quads`, and how that interacts with `VkPipelineRasterizationStateCreateInfo::frontFace`/`cullMode` translation the ordinary (non-tessellated) path already implements~~ (done, though the "systematic ... mismatch" framing was itself a red herring caught before landing the wrong fix: the pre-existing `Tessellator.cpp` `appendTriangle` Cw/Ccw operand-swap convention -- despite `TessellatorTest.cpp`'s own comment admitting it looks "not the standard convention" -- was tried and *reverted* after a real `deqp-vk` before/after comparison (`git stash`-ing just that change and rebuilding `feme_vulkan` for a fast A/B) showed it made `lower_left_domain`'s 8 cases newly pass while breaking `default_domain`/`upper_left_domain`'s previously-nearly-correct 16, an exact swap of which subgroup was broken rather than a net improvement -- proof the tessellator's own convention was already correct for the (implicitly assumed) upper-left domain origin. The real root cause: FeMe never parsed `VkTessellationDomainOrigin` at all (zero references to `DomainOrigin` anywhere in `feme/lib`/`feme/include`), silently treating every `VK_TESSELLATION_DOMAIN_ORIGIN_LOWER_LEFT` pipeline as upper-left -- and per the Vulkan spec (confirmed against `vktTessellationWindingTests.cpp`'s own `verifyResultImage`/`expectVisiblePrimitive` formula), the lower-left domain origin mirrors the tessellator's `(u,v)` parameter frame, which reverses every generated triangle's winding as a side effect of that mirror, independently of the tessellator's own (correct) Cw/Ccw convention. Fixed in `feme/lib/Vulkan/GraphicsPipeline.cpp`: `hasLowerLeftTessellationDomainOrigin` walks `VkPipelineTessellationStateCreateInfo::pNext` for a chained `VkPipelineTessellationDomainOriginStateCreateInfo`, returning true only when its `domainOrigin` is explicitly `VK_TESSELLATION_DOMAIN_ORIGIN_LOWER_LEFT` (the spec default, and an explicit `..._UPPER_LEFT`, both correctly leave it `false`); `flipTessellationWindingForDomainOrigin` swaps `TriangleCw`/`TriangleCcw` (leaving `Point`/`Line` untouched, since only a triangle has a Cw/Ccw-classified winding at all) and is applied to the merged `TessellationState::OutputPrimitive` at the very end of `compileGraphicsPipeline`, once, only when both a tessellation-control stage is present and the lower-left origin was requested. `GraphicsPipelineTest.{FlipsTessellationWindingForLowerLeftDomainOrigin,KeepsTessellationWindingForExplicitUpperLeftDomainOrigin}` cover both the flip and the "explicit upper-left behaves identically to omitted" case at the unit level, alongside the pre-existing `AcceptsTessellationStages`'s own unflipped-by-default assertion. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1833/1892 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`), up from 1831/1890 before this row (the two new tests). A real `deqp-vk` before/after comparison against `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*glsl*` (24 cases) confirms the fix: before, `lower_left_domain`'s 8 cases showed a genuine, complete front-face inversion (one pipeline of every `_ccw`/`_cw` pair rendering exactly the opposite of both pipelines in `default_domain`/`upper_left_domain`); after, all three domain-origin subgroups (`default_domain`/`lower_left_domain`/`upper_left_domain`) show the *identical* pattern -- confirming `lower_left_domain`'s own systematic inversion is gone. All 24 cases still `Fail` in CTS, but now uniformly for a distinct, much smaller defect (exactly 15/4096 stray pixels for quads, a 1-pixel row-fill boundary discrepancy for triangles, present only on the pipeline of each pair that is supposed to render visibly, never on the one that is supposed to be fully culled -- confirmed a rasterization-precision/tie-break issue, not a winding bug, since it is identical in every domain-origin subgroup and does not correlate with front-face or winding at all) -- spun off as new roadmap row H4j below, since it is a distinct rasterizer-correctness gap, not part of this row's own front-face/domain-origin scope. **Full group** (`dEQP-VK.tessellation.*`, 1114 cases) and the **regression sample** (`dEQP-VK.draw.*`, 1957-case sample) are both byte-identical to H4h's own recorded totals (8/227/879 and 12/139/1806 respectively) -- 0 regressions, expected, since `VkTessellationDomainOrigin` was previously silently ignored everywhere and this fix only changes behavior when a pipeline actually chains the lower-left struct. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed no change needed: `VkPipelineTessellationDomainOriginStateCreateInfo` is a core Vulkan 1.2 (originally `VK_KHR_maintenance2`) pipeline-creation struct, not a feature bit or an extension name that needs advertising -- it was always legal for an application to chain, FeMe just silently ignored its contents until now, the same "always-accepted struct now actually honored" shape as H4b/H4h's own tessellation-state parsing needed no inventory change either. | H4j | ~~**All 24 `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*glsl*` cases still fail even after H4i's own domain-origin fix, now uniformly at a small, distinct rasterization-precision defect**: exactly 15/4096 stray red pixels along what looks like a diagonal/seam pattern for `glsl_quads_*` (coordinates cluster near a corner and along a `y == x` diagonal, e.g. `(14,14),(15,15),(16,16),(17,17)` and `(45,45),(47,47),(55,55),(57,57)`, plus a small run near one corner like `(62,0),(63,0),(62,1),(61,2),(60,3),(59,4)`), and a 1-pixel-row-fill boundary discrepancy for `glsl_triangles_*` (`verifyResultImage`'s top/bottom-row white-pixel-count check is off by exactly one: 64/64 filled where 63 is expected, or 1/64 filled where 0 is expected). Only ever appears on the pipeline of each `_ccw`/`_cw` test-case pair that is supposed to render visibly (never the one supposed to be fully culled), identically across `default_domain`/`lower_left_domain`/`upper_left_domain` -- i.e. it does not correlate with front-face, winding, or domain origin at all, confirming it is a rasterizer tie-break/rounding or tessellator crack-free-bridging-seam issue, not a winding bug. Root cause not yet isolated -- candidates to investigate first: the tessellator's own "crack-free" boundary-ring-to-inset-core bridging (`Tessellator.cpp`'s `appendTriangleLattice`/`tessellateQuad`, whether the seam between the two regions is truly watertight at every sample point) and the rasterizer's own top-left fill-rule edge-ownership tie-break for two triangles sharing an exact edge (`Executor.cpp`'s `edgeFn`/coverage test)~~ (done -- both root causes were isolated entirely in `Executor.cpp`'s coverage test, not `Tessellator.cpp`'s bridging (the tessellator's own boundary-ring-to-inset-core seam was verified watertight by direct instrumentation: the two candidate triangles at a crack pixel share bit-identical vertex positions, confirming no duplicate/divergent domain-point evaluation). **Root cause 1 (quads, 15 stray pixels)**: the per-sample coverage test's `edgeFn` (`float`) is not bit-exactly antisymmetric under vertex-order reversal (its two directions subtract different operand pairs, `P - A` vs `P - B`), so two adjacent triangles sharing an exact diagonal edge (the tessellator's own quad-core-cell split) can each independently round a sample landing almost exactly on that shared edge to a spuriously negative value and both reject it -- a classic floating-point rasterization crack, confirmed via `FEME_DEBUG_XY`-gated instrumentation at CTS's own failing pixel (16,16) and reproduced in isolation with a Python float32 emulation. **Root cause 2 (triangles, 1-pixel row-fill off-by-one)**: an independent, pre-existing polarity bug in `isTopLeftEdge`: with tessellation factor 5 landing every domain edge on a clean multiple of `64/5 = 12.8`, a lone tessellated triangle's own outer hypotenuse can land a sample exactly on `E == 0.0`, and the old (backwards) tie-break formula (`(Dy == 0 && Dx > 0) || Dy < 0`) included it when Vulkan's top-left rule requires exclusion, independent of winding or domain origin (confirmed identical in both `_ccw` and `_cw` "visible" pipelines). **Fixes**: (1) a new `edgeFnD` (the same edge function, evaluated in `double`) replaces `edgeFn` for the coverage test's three inside/outside comparisons only (the barycentric interpolation weights stay `float`, since those are values, not coverage decisions) -- shrinking the rounding from `float`'s ~2^-23 relative precision to `double`'s ~2^-52, several orders of magnitude below any crack this rasterizer's own coordinate range produces; (2) `isTopLeftEdge`'s polarity is flipped to `(Dy == 0 && Dx < 0) || Dy > 0`, re-derived from and matching Vulkan's (D3D/OpenGL-identical) top-left convention for a positively-wound triangle (`Area > 0`, guaranteed by the existing `SArea < 0.0f` triangle-assembly reorder). `ExecutorTest.cpp` gains two new cases: `DoublePrecisionEdgeTestClosesAFloatRoundingCrackBetweenAdjacentTriangles` (two triangles sharing a non-axis-aligned, non-"nice"-fraction float32 edge reached through the executor's own NDC-to-screen `projectVertex` transform, confirmed to reproduce the exact crack pre-fix and close it post-fix) and `TopLeftTieBreakExcludesALoneTrianglesOwnBoundaryEdge` (a single triangle whose hypotenuse is a 4x4 viewport's own anti-diagonal, confirming all four boundary pixel centers landing exactly on it are excluded, leaving exactly the 6 strictly-interior pixels filled); both are confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code and pass against the fix. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1835/1894 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`), up from 1833/1892 before this row (the two new tests). A real `deqp-vk` run against `dEQP-VK.tessellation.winding.*glsl*` (24 cases) confirms the fix: **all 24/24 now pass** (up from 0/24 before this row). **Full group** (`dEQP-VK.tessellation.*`, 1114 cases) rises to 32/1114 pass (up from H4i's own 8/1114, exactly the 24 newly-fixed winding cases), 203/1114 fail (down from 227, the same 24), 879/1114 not-supported (unchanged) -- confirming zero regressions, an exact pass/fail shift matching this row's own scope. The **regression sample** (`dEQP-VK.draw.*`, 1957-case sample) is byte-identical to H4i's own recorded totals (12/139/1806) -- 0 regressions, expected, since neither fix changes any coverage-test outcome that was not already a genuine floating-point tie/crack. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed no change needed: this is a pure rasterizer-precision bug fix, touching no feature bit or extension. See "Roadmap H4j: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full before/after breakdown) | H4i | `feme/lib/Graphics/Executor.cpp` | P1 |

`GeometryWrapper.cpp` gains `lowerGeometryInvocationID` (sharing a new `lowerGeometryInvocationField` helper with the existing `lowerGeometryPrimitiveID`), wired into the `InputLoad` switch for `SignatureSystemValue::InvocationID` (which aliases hull's `OutputControlPointID` by enum value but is disambiguated by stage). `buildGeometryInvocations` (GeometryInputs.h/.cpp) gains an optional `InvocationIDs` array parameter, populating each record's new field. `Executor.cpp`'s H5d geometry-chaining block now computes `Invocations = max(GState.Invocations, 1)` and builds `RowCount = PrimitiveCount * Invocations` rows (each primitive's input vertex attributes repeated once per invocation, primitive-major/invocation-minor, i.e. row `= P * Invocations + Inv`), each stamped with both its `SV_PrimitiveID` and its own `gl_InvocationID`; `GeomInputLinks`'s varying-link filter now also excludes `InvocationID` (sourced from the invocation record, not a linked varying), mirroring `PrimitiveID`'s existing exclusion. `collectGeometryStreams`/`mergeGeometryStreamsInLaneOrder` needed zero logic changes -- both already treat `Args.PrimitiveCount` generically as "row count" and merge lanes in array order, confirmed by a real `ExecutorTest.cpp` case (`GeometryStageInvocationsRunOncePerDeclaredInvocationCount`, `Invocations = 2` on a single triangle, invocation-0/invocation-1 emit differently colored full-viewport strips, `BlendMode::Replace` requires invocation 1's strip to be the final write for the test to pass). | H5e-a | ~~**`ConvertSPIRVToLLVMPass`/`SPIRVToLLVMPatterns` have no conversion pattern for SPIR-V's `spirv.EmitVertex`/`spirv.EndPrimitive` ops at all** (confirmed absent by grepping the whole `lib/`/`include/` tree for `EmitVertexOp`/`EndPrimitiveOp`), the root cause behind 122 of H5e's own measured 167 `dEQP-VK.geometry.*` failures (`error: failed to legalize operation 'spirv.EmitVertex'`/`'spirv.EndPrimitive'` -- virtually every real GLSL geometry shader calls both, since without them a geometry stage can emit no output vertices at all). Needs a new pattern turning each op into a call to the same `feme.stage.stream.emit`/`feme.stage.stream.cut` intrinsics `GeometryWrapperPass::lowerGeometryStreamEmit`/`lowerGeometryStreamCut` (built under G5, already fully implemented and tested) already know how to lower, mirroring how every other stage-IO SPIR-V op already routes through a `feme.stage.*` intrinsic rather than a bespoke LLVM IR shape. A real `dEQP-VK.geometry.*` re-run after this row lands should re-triage the remaining, still-unexplained ~21-case "silent `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED`, no diagnostic emitted" bucket H5e's own report flagged (degenerate zero-emit shaders and the `triangle_strip_adjacency`/`basic_primitive` input-primitive-class shapes) once the dominant EmitVertex/EndPrimitive noise is gone, splitting out further lettered rows for whatever remains~~ (done: `SPIRVToLLVMPatterns.cpp` gains `EmitVertexConversionPattern`/`EndPrimitiveConversionPattern`, converting `spirv.EmitVertex`/`spirv.EndPrimitive` into a call to `feme.stage.stream.emit(0)`/`feme.stage.stream.cut(0)` respectively, mirroring `SubpassLoadPattern`'s existing precedent of calling a `feme.stage.*` function directly rather than adding a bespoke LLVM IR shape; the stream operand is always the constant `0`, matching both ops' own SPIR-V spec text restricting them to "only one stream present". | H5e-b | ~~**21 `dEQP-VK.geometry.*` cases fail `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` with `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED` and no diagnostic printed at all** (`builtin_variable.in_block.primitive_id_in`/`primitive_id_in_restarted`, `input.basic_primitive.{line_strip,line_strip_adjacency,triangle_fan}`, `input.triangle_strip_adjacency.vertex_count_*`, `emit.{line_strip,points,triangle_strip}_emit_0_end_0`) -- exactly H5e's own flagged bucket, unchanged in composition and count now that H5e-a's `EmitVertex`/`EndPrimitive` noise is gone. Root cause not yet isolated at all: needs bisecting which of `GraphicsPipeline.cpp`'s geometry-stage acceptance checks, `GeometryWrapperPass`, or a still-missing input-primitive-class/degenerate-zero-emit-shader code path silently rejects pipeline creation with no error text reaching the log~~ (done: two independent, unrelated false-positive validation bugs in `GraphicsPipeline.cpp` accounted for all 21 cases, both stale relative to work later milestones had already landed. (1) The creation-time `primitiveRestartEnable` gate still only allowed `VK_PRIMITIVE_TOPOLOGY_TRIANGLE_STRIP`, even though H5d's own `Executor.cpp` had long since implemented restart for every strip/fan topology (`LineStrip`/`TriangleFan`/the two `*StripWithAdjacency` topologies too) -- the two never agreed after H5d landed. Fixed by adding `feme::graphics::topologySupportsPrimitiveRestart` as the single shared source of truth both `GraphicsPipeline.cpp`'s gate and `Executor.cpp`'s own `RestartEnabled` condition now consult, accounting for 18 of the 21 cases. (2) `validateStageInterfaces`'s "the last pre-rasterization stage must write a 4-component SV_Position" check (and the fragment-input/vertex-output location-linkage loop right next to it) rejected a geometry entry point that emits no vertices at all (`dEQP-VK.geometry.emit.*_emit_0_end_0`'s degenerate `void main(void) {}` bodies): SPIR-V only lists an entry point's *used* interface variables, so such a shader's own reflected signature has zero elements at all, not merely a missing `Position`. Nothing is ever rasterized from a stage that emits nothing, regardless of whether it wrote a position or a varying, so both checks (and `Executor.cpp`'s own runtime counterpart of the first one) now treat an entirely-empty geometry signature as a legal no-op instead, accounting for the remaining 3 cases -- all three now pass outright. `FEME_VULKAN_LOG_CREATION_ERRORS=1` reruns of the exact 21-case list confirmed each fix in isolation; the full `dEQP-VK.geometry.*` group moves from 1/200 to 4/200 passing (166 -> 163 failing, 33/200 not-supported unchanged), with the 18 restart cases now correctly landing in H5e-a's own already-tracked, unrelated 60-case `getelementptr`/output-array-storage bucket instead of a silent, unattributed failure. `dEQP-VK.draw.*`'s regression sample is byte-identical to H5e-a's own baseline, 0 regressions. `GraphicsPipelineTest.cpp` gains `AcceptsPrimitiveRestartOnStripAndFanTopologies`/`AcceptsGeometryStageThatNeverEmits`, `PipelineTest.cpp` gains `SupportsPrimitiveRestartIdentifiesEveryStripAndFanKind`, and `ExecutorTest.cpp` gains `ExecutesDrawsAsNoOpWhenGeometryStageNeverEmits`) | H5e-a | `feme/lib/Vulkan/GraphicsPipeline.cpp`, `feme/lib/Transforms/CPU/GeometryWrapper.cpp` | P1 |
 |
| H5e-c | ~~**18 `dEQP-VK.geometry.layered.{1d_array,2d_array}.*` cases (`multiple_layers_per_invocation`, `render_to_one`, `render_to_default_layer`, `render_to_all`) fail at `vkQueueSubmit` with `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED`**, newly exposed by H5e-a (previously masked by the `EmitVertex`/`EndPrimitive` legalization failure). Distinct from H5e-b's pipeline-creation-time failure and from the pre-existing `layered.3d` image-creation gap (`vk.createImage`, unrelated) and `layered.*.fragment_layer` fragment-input gap (`feme-cpu-wrap-fragment`, unrelated) -- this bucket is specific to a layered geometry-stage draw's own execution, using `gl_Layer` output from the geometry stage rather than just varying output-array storage. Root cause not yet isolated~~ (done, with a broader scope than this row's own "1d_array,2d_array" framing claimed: root cause is `RenderPass.cpp`'s `resolveAttachmentView`, which rejected any render-target view dimension other than 2D/2D-array outright -- Vulkan also permits `VK_IMAGE_VIEW_TYPE_1D`/`_1D_ARRAY`/`_CUBE`/`_CUBE_ARRAY` as a color attachment, and none of the four had ever been accepted, so `dEQP-VK.geometry.layered.cube.*`/`cube_array.*` hit the identical failure this row never listed, alongside the named `1d_array`/`2d_array` groups -- 18 cases total either way, just a different 18 than the row's own text enumerated (6 `1d_array` + 6 `cube` + 6 `cube_array`, not 6 `1d_array` + 12 `2d_array`; the `2d_array` cases this row expected never failed at `vkQueueSubmit` at all, since `Texture2DArray` was already accepted -- they instead already landed in H5e-e's own "Rendered images are incorrect" bucket, which is where all 18 of *this* row's cases now land too). Nothing about the gap was geometry-stage-specific: any draw into a 1D/cube(-array) render target hit it, a geometry stage's `gl_Layer` output was incidental. Fixed by accepting all four additional dimensions in `resolveAttachmentView`, addressed identically to the already-accepted 2D/2D-array case (`Image` never itself has a "1D"/"cube" dimension of its own -- only the *view's* `ImageDimension` records the addressing convention, which the existing layer-major addressing already honors with no further change). Along the way, `vkQueueSubmit`'s own error path (`Sync.cpp`) was found to discard its `llvm::Error` with a bare `consumeError` rather than routing it through the existing `feme::vulkan::logCreationFailure` opt-in diagnostic `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` already uses -- fixed first, which is what let `FEME_VULKAN_LOG_CREATION_ERRORS=1` actually surface `resolveAttachmentView`'s own message ("only a 2D or 2D-array image view may be a render target") and confirm the root cause directly rather than by inference. A real `dEQP-VK.geometry.*` re-run (200 cases) confirms all 18 targeted cases now run to completion (0 `vkQueueSubmit` failures remain), reclassified into H5e-e's own already-tracked bucket (6 -> 24 cases); group totals are unchanged (4 Pass / 163 Fail / 33 NotSupported), 0 regressions. `dEQP-VK.draw.*`'s 1957-case sample is byte-identical to every prior row's own baseline (12/155/1790), 0 regressions. `RenderPassTest.cpp` gains `ResolveAttachmentViewAcceptsOneDArrayView`, `ResolveAttachmentViewAcceptsCubeArrayView`, and `ResolveAttachmentViewRejects3DView`. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1870/1929 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`), up from H5e-b's own 1867/1926 by exactly the 3 new unit tests this row adds; see "Roadmap H5e-c: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H5e-a | `feme/lib/Vulkan/RenderPass.cpp`, `feme/lib/Vulkan/Sync.cpp` | P1 |
| H5e-d | ~~**6 `dEQP-VK.geometry.emit.*_emit_0_end_1` cases fail with `feme-cpu-wrap-geometry: geometry stage wrapper requires attached feme.signature metadata`**, newly exposed by H5e-a. A geometry entry point compiled from one of these specific `emit`-count shapes (a shader that ends its primitive without emitting on that particular stream/count combination) reaches `GeometryWrapperPass` without the `feme.signature` metadata it requires to construct the wrapper -- a reflection/metadata-attachment gap somewhere upstream of `GeometryWrapperPass` itself (likely `CanonicalizeStagePass` or SPIR-V import), not `GeometryWrapperPass`'s own lowering (which is otherwise proven correct by G5's existing test suite and this row's own `EmitVertex`/`EndPrimitive` fix). Root cause not yet isolated~~ (done -- root-caused to `canonicalizeSPIRVStage` (`feme/lib/Transforms/Graphics/CanonicalizeStage.cpp`) itself, the same layer roadmap H4g's own tessellation investigation flagged as a recurring shape: its signature-building branch was (and, for Vertex/Fragment, still is) scoped to `if (!InputGlobals.empty() || !OutputGlobals.empty())`, so a geometry entry compiled from an `emit_0_end_{1,2}` shape -- whose `emitCountA == 0` loop body (the only code that reads `gl_in[]`/writes `gl_Position`/etc.) never emits a single instruction, leaving only a bare `EndPrimitive()` call, already lowered to `feme.stage.stream.cut` by H5e-a's own `SPIRVToLLVMPatterns` fix -- discovers zero stage-IO globals and never reaches `dxil::setEntrySignature`, leaving no `!feme.signature` metadata for `GeometryWrapperPass` to find. Fixed with a new `else if (Stage == ShaderStage::Geometry)` branch attaching an explicit empty `EntrySignature` whenever the existing branch does not fire, scoped to `Geometry` only (unlike Vertex/Fragment, a geometry entry is only ever routed through `canonicalizeSPIRVStage`, never `canonicalizeDXILStage`, per `CanonicalizeStagePass::run`'s own dispatch, so there is no DXIL-origin-entry ambiguity to preserve here the way H4g's own broader attempt ran into and reverted) -- `lowerGeometryStageOps` only ever looks an `ElementID` up in `Sig` for an actual input-load/output-store/stream-emit call, none of which this shape has, so an empty signature is exactly correct, not just tolerated. Covered by a new `CanonicalizeStageTest.GeometryStreamCutOnlyEntryStillGetsASignature` unit test (a geometry entry with only a bare `feme.stage.stream.cut` call and no stage-IO globals at all, confirming it now gets an attached, empty `!feme.signature`). `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1871/1930 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`), up from H5e-c's own 1870/1929 by exactly the 1 new unit test this row adds. A real `deqp-vk` run confirms the fix end to end: all 3 `dEQP-VK.geometry.emit.*_emit_0_end_1` cases now pass (up from 0/3), and a full `dEQP-VK.geometry.*` re-run (200 cases) finds exactly 6 new passes -- the 3 `_emit_0_end_1` cases this row's own text names plus the 3 `_emit_0_end_2` siblings sharing the identical `emitCountA == 0` shape (`emit_0_end_0`'s own 3 cases were already fixed by H5e-b) -- rising to 10/200 pass, 157/200 fail (down from 163), 33/200 not-supported (unchanged), 0 regressions. The `dEQP-VK.draw.*` 1957-case regression sample is byte-identical to H5e-c's own recorded totals (12/155/1790) -- 0 regressions, expected, since no `dEQP-VK.draw.*` case in that sample exercises a geometry stage at all. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed no change needed: this is a pure reflection/metadata-attachment fix, touching no feature bit or extension. See "Roadmap H5e-d: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H5e-a | `feme/lib/Transforms/Graphics/CanonicalizeStage.cpp`, `feme/lib/Transforms/CPU/GeometryWrapper.cpp` | P2 |
| H5e-e | ~~**6 `dEQP-VK.geometry.layered.2d_array.*.multiple_layers_per_invocation` variants render an incorrect image** (`Fail (Rendered images are incorrect)`), newly exposed by H5e-a. Unlike H5e-b/H5e-c's outright creation/submission failures, these pipelines now run to completion without error and produce a wrong result -- a genuine rendering-correctness bug in the geometry-to-layered-render-target path (most likely `gl_Layer` routing, per-invocation output-vertex addressing, or stream-merge ordering under `Invocations > 1`, none yet isolated). Root cause not yet isolated~~ (done, with a broader scope than this row's own "6 `2d_array`" framing claimed: the actual bucket this row owns is 24 cases -- `render_to_one`, `render_to_default_layer`, `multiple_layers_per_invocation` across all 4 non-`3d` view types (`1d_array`/`2d_array`/`cube`/`cube_array`) x 2 sizes -- and it was two separate, unrelated `CommandBuffer.cpp` bugs, neither one actually `gl_Layer` routing/per-invocation addressing/stream-merge ordering as this row's own text suspected. (1) `applyLoadOps`'s `ViewMask` fallback for a non-multiview attachment (`Binding.ViewMask == 0`) collapsed to a single bit (`1u`) regardless of the attachment's real layer count, so `LOAD_OP_CLEAR` on a layered (non-multiview) attachment only ever cleared layer 0, leaving every other layer's backing memory uninitialized garbage -- fixed with a new `fullLayerMask(Layers)` helper. (2) `runDraw`'s per-view loop unconditionally called `sliceAttachmentLayer(A, ViewIndex)` on every attachment for every draw, including the ordinary non-multiview case (`ViewIndex` always 0), permanently slicing every attachment down to exactly 1 layer before `Executor.cpp`'s own already-correct per-primitive `gl_Layer`/`resolveRenderTargetArrayLayer` routing ever got a chance to see more than that one layer -- any primitive routed to a non-zero layer was silently discarded as out of range. Fixed by only performing that per-view slicing when `Gfx.Binding.ViewMask != 0` (true multiview); a plain layered (non-multiview) draw now sees its attachments' full, unsliced layer range, exactly as `Executor.cpp` already expected. New `DrawTest.GeometryStageLayerOutputRoutesToANonMultiviewLayer` (Vulkan unit test), covering both fixes at once: a real `EmitVertex`/`EndPrimitive`-driven geometry shader routing `gl_Layer = 1` into a two-layer, non-multiview render target, confirming layer 0 reads back `LOAD_OP_CLEAR`'s own black (not garbage) and layer 1 reads back the fragment stage's red (not discarded). Confirmed to fail without either fix individually. A real `dEQP-VK.geometry.layered.*` re-run (100 cases) goes from 0/100 Pass (H5e-c's own baseline) to 24/100 Pass -- exactly this bucket's own 24 target cases, 0 regressions, `3d`'s pre-existing unrelated `vkCreateImage` gap untouched. A real `dEQP-VK.geometry.*` re-run (200 cases) goes from 10/200 (H5e-d's own baseline) to 34/200 Pass, 0 regressions. `dEQP-VK.draw.*`'s 1957-case sample goes from 12/1957 to 14/1957 Pass (2 new: `shader_layer.vertex_shader_5`'s `renderpass`/`partial_secondary_cmd_buff` variants, which route a vertex-shader-written `gl_Layer` the same way, confirming fix (2) is not geometry-stage-specific), 0 regressions. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full, 1872/1931 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`), up from H5e-d's own 1871/1930 by exactly the 1 new unit test this row adds; see "Roadmap H5e-e: measured impact" in VulkanCTSReport.md for the full breakdown) | H5e-a | `feme/lib/Vulkan/CommandBuffer.cpp` | P2 |

Whole `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader` group (partially done, broken down below the same way H4/H5 were: H6a closes the execution-mode half of "reflect a mesh entry point at all"; H6b-H6i track the remaining canonicalization, CPU-lowering, executor-chaining, Vulkan-API-acceptance and CTS-triage work an investigation mirroring H5's own found necessary before landing; fixing that lets 81 of the 82 formerly-blocked cases progress further, leaving only a pre-existing sampled-image/sampler combined-handle rejection and surfacing a new dominant blocker in their place, tracked as H6g-b-a-i-a-i-a (closed: root-caused and fixed a `feme.cpu.masked.store.*` divergent-vector-value consumer gap in `checkVectorDecompositionSupported`/`widenMaskedStore` -- fixing it lets 68 of the 148 formerly-blocked cases progress further, incidentally also closes H6g-b-b (the same code path, mischaracterized there as an "aggregate" masked-store value), and surfaces a new dominant blocker in their place, tracked as H6g-b-a-i-a-i-b, a divergent vector value used as an `fcmp`/`icmp` comparison operand))), and H6g-b-c (closed: wired `ShaderStage::Mesh` into `ValidateStagePass::run`, so this unresolved arrayed-builtin-block access -- left unrewritten by `H6c-a-a-iii`'s own fix, previously reaching the JIT as a genuinely undefined symbol -- is now diagnosed cleanly at compile time instead; its own re-run of `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.builtin.*` surfaces one further, narrower blocker of its own, tracked as H6l) -- H6g-b-a-i-a-i-c's own re-run of that same bucket found its 80 previously-JIT-symbol-blocked cases split evenly between the already-tracked H6g-b-c and a new `MeshOutputWrapperPass` catch-all rejection, tracked (one level under H6 rather than nested any deeper under H6g-b, per the standing instruction against nesting milestone IDs more than one lowercase letter deep going forward) as H6g-b-d (closed: root-caused and fixed a `lowerMeshStageOps` bug of its own -- its catch-all rejected *any* leftover call that was not itself one of the two shapes it lowers, including completely unrelated, perfectly ordinary calls a mesh entry legitimately still makes (e.g. its own `feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.*` buffer read feeding an output store's value), not only a genuinely-unlowered `feme.stage.*` op -- fixing it lets the 40 previously-blocked cases progress further, uncovering a new `vkQueueSubmit` vertex-output/fragment-input interface-mismatch bug in their place, tracked as H6j (closed: root-caused and fixed a `CanonicalizeStage.cpp` signature-reflection gap of its own -- a mesh entry's own plain per-vertex output global folded its outer per-vertex array dimension into `RowCount` instead of peeling it off, disagreeing with the fragment stage's own unarrayed input at the same location -- fixing it lets those 40 cases progress further, uncovering a new `SIGSEGV`/`SIGABRT` heap-corruption crash inside `executeDraws` itself in 32 of them in their place, not yet root-caused, tracked as H6k)) -- milestone remains open, depending on H6g-b-a-i-a-i-b, H6l and H6k) | G6 | `feme/lib/Vulkan/{GraphicsPipeline,CommandBuffer,PhysicalDeviceInfo}.cpp`, `feme/lib/Graphics` | P2 |

| H6c-a-b | ~~**Wire `TaskPayloadBuilder` into a task entry's canonicalized payload-store operation**, once `TaskPayloadWorkgroupEXT` has an address-space convention and import pattern (H6h), `CanonicalizeStagePass::run` accepts `ShaderStage::Amplification` and canonicalizes a payload write into a new `feme.stage.*` op (H6i), and a task entry's checked `EmitMeshTasksEXT` dispatch queue (H6d) gives the payload somewhere real to be read from by the mesh workgroups it dispatches~~ (done: new `feme::cpu::TaskPayloadWrapperPass` (`TaskPayloadWrapper.h`/`.cpp`), `MeshOutputWrapperPass`'s deliberately simpler task-stage counterpart, runs immediately before `EntryWrapperPass` in `Pipeline.cpp`'s `ShaderStage::Amplification` case; it appends two new named wave-body parameters (`task_payload`, `task_max_payload_bytes`) and lowers every masked payload store into a store at `task_payload + offset` -- since a task payload store's `offset` operand is always the same compile-time constant for every lane at one call site (unlike a mesh output store's dynamic `Vertex` operand), every lane targets one shared address, needing only the same load-select-store pattern `MeshOutputWrapper.cpp` already uses and no `FemeStageLayout`/element-ID lookup at all; a defensive `Offset + ByteSize <= MaxPayloadBytes` bounds check is ANDed into each lane's mask, mirroring `MeshOutputWrapper.cpp`'s own clamping precedent. `StageMaskCalls`/`Linearize`/`SIMDize` gained the matching masked-call plumbing (`createMaskedTaskPayloadStore`, a `TaskPayloadStore` case in `applyStageMasks`/`hasStageMaskOps`, `widenMaskedTaskPayloadStore`); new `TaskArgsField`/`getTaskArgsType` (`StageArgsLayout.h`) mirror `FemeTaskArgs`'s exact C layout; `EntryWrapperPass` gained an `IsTask`-gated `ArgsTy`/`WrapperEnv` extension mirroring H6c-a-a's own `IsMesh` one exactly (all three wrapper-building call sites updated, both flags threaded through consistently, never both true at once). Host side: `TaskPayloadBuilder` gained a `getMutableBytes()` accessor (the only way its storage picks up a real compiled write); `GraphicsPipeline::setMeshStage` gained a `MaxTaskPayloadBytes` parameter populated from a new `feme::vulkan::MaxTaskPayloadBytes` constant (`GraphicsPipeline.h`, replacing `EntryPoints.cpp`'s previously-hardcoded `maxTaskPayloadSize` literal so the two can never disagree); `Executor::executeDraws`'s task-dispatch loop now constructs one `TaskPayloadBuilder` per task workgroup, backs `TaskResources::Payload` with its mutable bytes before `invokeTask`, and threads the same bytes into every mesh workgroup its `EmitMeshTasksEXT` request dispatches, replacing the two hardcoded `/*Payload=*/{}` call sites H6c-a-a's own row left in place. | H6d | ~~**Implement checked amplification dispatch queues and meshlet assembly**: a task entry's `EmitMeshTasksEXT` becomes a bounded, validated dispatch of mesh workgroups (mirroring the compute dispatch queue's own bounds checks), and a mesh workgroup's emitted per-vertex/per-primitive outputs become one meshlet the executor can consume, with topology validation (an out-of-range vertex index in a primitive's index list is diagnosed, not read out of bounds)~~ (done: new `feme::graphics::AmplificationDispatchQueue`/`AmplificationDispatch.h` -- `AmplificationDispatchQueue::create` validates a requested `EmitMeshTasksEXT(x, y, z)` group count against a per-dimension bound (`AmplificationDispatchLimits::MaxGroupCount`, mirroring `maxComputeWorkGroupCount`'s own per-dimension check in `feme/lib/Vulkan/CommandBuffer.cpp`'s `validateGroupCount`) and a total-product bound (`MaxTotalGroupCount`, mesh shading's own `maxMeshWorkGroupTotalCount`, which compute's own dispatch has no equivalent of), computing the product in 64 bits so an over-large per-dimension request cannot wrap back into range; a validated queue then enumerates each dispatched mesh workgroup's group ID (`getGroupID`, X-fastest/Z-slowest, mirroring `feme::cpu::runDispatch`'s own triple loop) rather than eagerly invoking anything, since `Executor::executeDraws` (H6e) needs to interleave that enumeration with per-workgroup mesh state. New `feme::graphics::Meshlet`/`Meshlet.h` and `assembleMeshlet` (`MeshOutputBuilder` -> `Meshlet`) trim a mesh workgroup's completed `MeshOutputBuilder` down to its actual (`SetMeshOutputsEXT`-declared) vertex/primitive counts and *re-validate* every primitive's vertex index list against that actual vertex count at assembly time, independently of `MeshOutputBuilder::setPrimitiveIndices`'s own write-time check (H6c) -- necessary because a real compiled workgroup's output, once wired directly into the builder's storage (H6c-a-a), will not necessarily have gone through that checked setter, so an out-of-range vertex index must still be diagnosed (an `llvm::Error` naming the offending primitive and index) rather than read out of bounds when the executor later walks it. 9 new unit tests (`AmplificationDispatchTest.cpp`'s 6, `MeshletTest.cpp`'s 3) cover today's scope. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build) passes in full; `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.*` stays 0/0/28044 (correctly so: nothing here touches SPIR-V import, canonicalization, or any Vulkan-facing path) and the `dEQP-VK.draw.*` 1957-case regression sample stays byte-identical to H6c's own recorded totals. new `GraphicsPipeline::setMeshStage`/`hasMeshStages`/`getMeshStage`/`hasTaskStage`/`getTaskStage`/`getMeshState` (`Pipeline.h`/`.cpp`, mirroring `setGeometryStage`) require a non-null mesh stage but allow a null task stage (direct mesh dispatch, no amplification); new `MeshDrawCommand`/`PreparedDraw::MeshDraws` (`PreparedDraw.h`) carry either a direct mesh-dispatch shape or a task-dispatch shape's `GroupCount`. `Executor::executeDraws` itself: gates the vertex-topology validation switch and the unconditional vertex-stage signature fetch behind `!Pipeline.hasMeshStages()`; validates `Draw.Draws`/`Draw.MeshDraws` are mutually exclusive; derives `RasterClass` from `Mesh.OutputTopology` when mesh stages are bound; and adds a dispatch-and-rasterize branch that, per `MeshDrawCommand`, either enumerates every mesh workgroup directly (no task stage bound) or first runs each task workgroup (X-fastest order, mirroring `feme::cpu::runDispatch`) to read back `FemeTaskArgs::MeshGroupCount` and build a per-task-workgroup `AmplificationDispatchQueue` from it (task stage bound) -- either way, every enumerated mesh workgroup is invoked via `CompiledStage::invokeMesh`, its raw output flattened into a `MeshOutputBuilder` and trimmed to a `Meshlet` via `assembleMeshlet` (H6d), and all resulting meshlets (in dispatch order) merged into one `StageStorage` plus absolute triangle/line index lists fed to `RasterizePrimitives`. Two deliberate, documented simplifications carried by this row alone (not real ABI-matching work, since nothing yet writes real mesh/task output -- see below): a self-consistent (not Vulkan-spec-mandated) `flattenMeshRow`/`unflattenMeshRow` scheme converting between raw `StageStorage` and `MeshOutputBuilder`/`Meshlet` rows in `MeshSig`'s declared Output order; and a placeholder `AmplificationDispatchLimits` (`MaxGroupCount={65535,65535,65535}`, `MaxTotalGroupCount=4194304`) standing in for H6f's own real `VkPhysicalDeviceMeshShaderPropertiesEXT`-sourced bounds. With `H6c-a-a-iii` landed, a real full `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.*` re-run (28044 cases, resume-loop methodology) reached these same 235+33 cases and found the *majority* of the 235 `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` failures (`vkRefUtil.cpp:37`) traced to a bug this row itself introduced no fix for yet at the time: `translateGraphicsPipeline`'s own mesh-stage carve-out (`GraphicsPipeline.cpp`) rejected any non-null `pVertexInputState`/`pInputAssemblyState` on a mesh pipeline outright with `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED` -- but the Vulkan spec (`VkGraphicsPipelineCreateInfo::pVertexInputState`/`pInputAssemblyState`'s own doc comments, `VK_EXT_mesh_shader`/`VK_NV_mesh_shader` carve-out) only requires both to be *ignored* when the pipeline includes a mesh shader stage, not null -- and `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.*` itself builds every one of its pipelines through the shared, non-mesh-aware `vkObjUtil.cpp` `makeGraphicsPipeline` helper, which unconditionally passes a non-null default `pVertexInputState`/`pInputAssemblyState` regardless of stage set. Fixed by simply not reading either pointer for a mesh pipeline, per the spec's own "ignored" wording (`GraphicsPipelineTest.cpp`'s `RejectsMeshPipelineWith{VertexInput,InputAssembly}State` replaced with `AcceptsMeshPipelineWith{VertexInput,InputAssembly}State`, now expecting `VK_SUCCESS`). `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build): **2009/2009** discovered, **1950** passing (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`) -- unchanged from `H6c-a-a-iii`'s own baseline, since this row replaces 2 existing tests rather than adding new ones. | H6g-b-a-i | ~~**`ConvertSPIRVToLLVMPass` fails to legalize `spirv.AccessChain` outright ("failed to legalize operation 'spirv.AccessChain' that was explicitly marked illegal")**, now the single dominant cause found within H6g-b-a's own newly-shrunk 218-case `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`/`vkRefUtil.cpp:37` bucket (80 of 218 cases still failing there hit exactly this, per a diagnostic-logged re-run of that bucket alone), and newly reachable only now that H6g-b-a's own fix lets `PerPrimitiveEXT`-decorated per-primitive-block SPIR-V actually deserialize far enough to reach legalization at all. Root cause not yet isolated (which `spirv.AccessChain` shape the existing conversion patterns don't cover -- a per-primitive-block member access through a `PerPrimitiveEXT`-decorated pointer specifically, given this row's own prerequisite, or something broader -- and whether the fix belongs in a new/extended conversion pattern or a legalization-target adjustment)~~ (done: root-caused to the upstream MLIR SPIR-V dialect, not any `PerPrimitiveEXT`-specific gap or `feme`-local pattern -- the actual base pointer type on every one of the 80 failing `spirv.AccessChain` ops was a generic `StorageBuffer`/`Block`-decorated struct holding ordinary vertex-attribute data (`vec2`/`vec3`/`vec4` float/int arrays of 4 elements each), not mesh-specific content at all; it only surfaces here because H6g-b-a's own fix is the first thing to let this SPIR-V shape reach legalization in this test group. `convertArrayType` (`mlir/lib/Conversion/SPIRVToLLVM/SPIRVToLLVM.cpp`) validated a declared `ArrayStride` against the element type's compact `SPIRVType::getSizeInBytes()` (12 bytes for `vector<3xf32>`), but the Vulkan spec requires an array's stride to be a multiple of its element's *base alignment* (16 bytes for a 3- or 4-component vector of 4-byte scalars, not its raw/compact size) -- so every conformant SPIR-V producer (glslang included) emits `ArrayStride=16` for `array<N x vec3<f32>>`, and this compact-size check spuriously rejected all of them as having an "unnatural" stride. A second, independently-triggerable copy of the same flaw was found in `VulkanLayoutUtils::decorateType(spirv::ArrayType, ...)` (`mlir/lib/Dialect/SPIRV/Utils/LayoutUtils.cpp`, shared by `SPIRVCompositeTypeLayoutPass` and `convertStructTypeWithOffset`'s own struct-identity check), which likewise computed the canonical stride and total array size from the raw compact element size instead of the alignment-rounded one. Fixed by adding `VulkanLayoutUtils::getNaturalArrayStride(Type)` (rounds the element's size up to its own alignment) and wiring it into both `convertArrayType`'s validity check and `decorateType(spirv::ArrayType, ...)`'s stride/size computation. New positive/negative lit tests: `mlir/test/Conversion/SPIRVToLLVM/spirv-types-to-llvm.mlir` (`array_with_natural_vector3_stride`, a `!spirv.array<4 x vector<3xf32>, stride=16>` now converting), `spirv-types-to-llvm-invalid.mlir` (`array_with_unnatural_vector3_stride`, confirming stride=12 for the same element -- the wrong, unrounded value -- is still correctly rejected), `mlir/test/Dialect/SPIRV/Transforms/layout-decoration.mlir` (a struct with an undecorated `array<4xvector<3xf32>>` member now decorated with `stride=16` and the following member correctly offset at 64, not 48), and `feme/test/Conversion/SPIRVToLLVM/spirv-to-llvm-storage-buffer.mlir` (`read_vec3_array_element`, a `StorageBuffer` block with a fixed-size `vec3` array member converting to the expected `llvm.spv.resource.getpointer`/GEP/load sequence, mirroring the file's existing `rtarray` cases). `ninja check-mlir-target-spirv check-mlir-dialect-spirv check-mlir-conversion-spirvtollvm`: 23/23, 52/52, 58/58 passing (all previously-passing tests unaffected, all new tests pass); the existing `MLIRSPIRVToLLVMTests` gtest unit suite: 3/3 unaffected. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build): unchanged at 1950/2009 passing (59 pre-existing `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`) -- this row replaces no existing test, only adds a new `RUN`-split case to an existing file. A real, diagnostic-logged re-run of the exact 218-case `vkRefUtil.cpp:37` bucket this row's own text named, through feme's actual Vulkan ICD, confirms the fix directly: **0** `spirv.AccessChain` legalization failures (down from 80), with the 80 formerly-blocked cases now progressing further and the group's own re-run finding a *new* dominant cause among them, `spirv.All` failing to legalize (81 of 218 cases, up from a pre-existing, already out-of-scope 1), tracked as new row H6g-b-a-i-a below; the remaining, already-tracked-out-of-scope 11 `spirv.MemoryBarrier`/4 `spirv.AtomicExchange`/1 `spirv.EXT.EmitMeshTasks`/1 `spirv.Any` cases are unaffected either way. `dEQP-VK.draw.*`'s 1957-case `draw_sample.txt` regression sample stays byte-identical (14 passed / 153 failed / 1790 not supported), **0 regressions**. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed no change needed: this is a pure MLIR SPIR-V layout/type-conversion correctness fix, touching no `feme`-advertised feature bit or extension. | H6g-b-a-i-a-i | ~~**`feme::cpu::UnsupportedOps` rejects a register-bound resource handle that survives to the FeMe CPU target's normalization pass ("unsupported raised operation: ... is a register-bound resource handle the FeMe CPU target cannot normalize into a heap access or the root-constant block ...")**, now the single dominant cause found within H6g-b-a-i-a's own 218-case `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`/`vkRefUtil.cpp:37` bucket (82 of 218 cases hit exactly this, per a diagnostic-logged re-run of that bucket alone, up from a pre-existing, already out-of-scope 1 -- the 81 cases H6g-b-a-i-a's own fix newly unblocked progress into this same failure), and newly reachable only now that H6g-b-a-i-a's own fix lets the `spirv.All`/`spirv.Any`-bearing SPIR-V this bucket's cases share actually legalize far enough to reach `feme-cpu`'s own resource-handle normalization pass. Root cause not yet isolated (which resource-declaration shape in this content the pass's existing "finite, unambiguous traditional binding, bindless access, or root-constant" recognition doesn't cover, and whether the fix belongs in `UnsupportedOps`/`RootConstantLowering` itself or an earlier canonicalization step feeding it)~~ (done: root-caused to `feme::cpu::SPIRVResourceLoweringPass`, not `UnsupportedOps` or `RootConstantLowering`. The dominant failing handles are the fragment-stage `spirv.VulkanBuffer` values produced for glslang's direct `layout(set=0,binding=*) std430 readonly buffer { ... }` blocks: `ConvertSPIRVToLLVMPass` had already lowered each SPIR-V `AccessChain` into `llvm.spv.resource.getpointer` selecting a constant struct field followed by ordinary LLVM `getelementptr` array indexing, but `SPIRVResourceLowering.cpp`'s pre-existing `collectHandles`/`hasOnlySupportedUses` logic only accepted a flat direct load/store user of the `getpointer` result. Any further GEP into the selected field therefore made the whole function fail the pass's all-or-nothing handle classification, leaving every handle in that function to survive into `UnsupportedOps`. Fixed locally and surgically in `SPIRVResourceLowering.cpp`: struct-typed `spirv.VulkanBuffer` handles in SPIR-V `StorageBuffer` storage class are now classified separately from uniform-buffer/root-constant shapes, ordinary constant/affine GEP chains rooted at their `llvm.spv.resource.getpointer` result are validated and lowered recursively by folding the GEP byte offset into the raw resource byte offset, and the final load/store is rewritten to the existing `feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.*`/`store.raw.*` helpers. The still-intentionally-rejected negative shape (a dynamic top-level field selector on a direct storage block) stays rejected and is locked down explicitly. New unit coverage in `feme/unittests/Transforms/CPU/SPIRVResourceLoweringTest.cpp`: `LowersDirectStorageBlockFieldAndNestedArrayAccessToResourceLoad`, `LowersStructuredStorageBufferFieldAccessToFieldOffset`, and `LeavesDirectStorageBlockDynamicFieldSelectorUnchanged`; the existing lit regression `feme/test/Transforms/CPU/spirv-resource-lowering-unsupported.ll` updated so its `field_access` case now checks for the lowered raw-resource load while the still-unsupported image/sampler and unbounded-descriptor-array cases remain untouched. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build): 1953/2012 passing, 59 pre-existing `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed` (up from 1950/2009 by exactly the 3 new unit tests; the adjusted lit file adds no discovered tests). A real ICD re-run of the exact 218-case `vkRefUtil.cpp:37` bucket confirms the named cause is cleared almost completely: the previous row's own 82 register-bound-handle rejections drop to **1** (the lone remainder is a pre-existing sampled-image/sampler combined-handle shape, already out of this row's storage-buffer scope), and the 81 newly-unblocked storage-buffer cases now progress further. Re-running the bucket with a combined stdout/stderr log and classifying each case by the first emitted FeMe/MLIR diagnostic when present yields 148 `feme-cpu-simdize` divergent-vector-value rejections, 15 `feme-cpu-wrap-mesh-output` metadata failures, 13 `feme-cpu-linearize` unsupported-control-flow-shape failures, 11 `spirv.MemoryBarrier` legalization failures, 7 specialization-constant failures, 5 `Symbols not found` failures already tracked by H6g-b-c, 4 `spirv.AtomicExchange` legalization failures, 2 `feme-cpu-wrap-fragment` metadata failures, 1 `spirv.EXT.EmitMeshTasks` legalization failure, the lone remaining sampled-image/sampler `UnsupportedOps` rejection, and 11 still-failing cases that emitted no FeMe/MLIR diagnostic before `deqp-vk`'s own Linux-only device-fault-test abort path triggered. `dEQP-VK.draw.*`'s 1957-case `draw_sample.txt` regression sample is byte-identical to H6g-b-a-i-a's own baseline (14 passed / 153 failed / 1790 not supported), with **0 regressions** and an identical per-case status map. `FeMeCPUDesign.md` needs no update: this is a completeness fix within the design's existing resource-normalization scope, not a change to the documented resource model. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` also need no update: no advertised feature bit or extension changes. | H6g-b-a-i-a-i-a | ~~**`feme-cpu-simdize` rejects a newly-unblocked divergent vector value in the same 218-case `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`/`vkRefUtil.cpp:37` bucket ("function 'main' has a divergent vector value ... used outside a supported insertelement-chain/resource-store/extractelement/select/shufflevector/phi/elementwise pattern; component decomposition is not yet supported for this use")**, now the new dominant first-emitted FeMe/MLIR diagnostic in that bucket once H6g-b-a-i-a-i's own direct-storage-buffer-handle fix lets those shaders progress further (148 of 218 cases in a combined stdout/stderr diagnostic rerun land here first; H6g-b-a-i-a-i reduced its own named `UnsupportedOps` bucket from 82 cases to a lone, out-of-scope sampled-image/sampler remainder). Root cause not yet isolated: needs a real failing shader/IR reduction to identify which divergent-vector use shape in the newly-unblocked mesh/fragment content is still outside `SIMDizePass`'s supported decomposition patterns, and whether the right fix belongs in `SIMDize.cpp` itself or in an earlier canonicalization/legalization pass feeding it~~ (done: reduced a real failing case (`dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.api.draw.*`) down to its exact IR shape by temporarily instrumenting `checkVectorDecompositionSupported`'s own rejection with a one-off diagnostic dump of the offending instruction and its users, then re-running that single case through the real ICD -- the dominant shape is a mesh entry point's own `gl_PrimitiveTriangleIndicesEXT[...] = uvec3(...)` write: unlike an ordinary per-vertex/per-primitive output element store, `PrimitiveIndices` has no canonicalized `feme.stage.*` op of its own to become a `feme.cpu.resource.*`/masked-output-store call instead (see `MeshOutputWrapper.h`'s own "Left open by this row" note), so it survives as an ordinary `store` of a divergent `<3 x i32>` value that `feme::cpu::LinearizePass` masks into a `feme.cpu.masked.store.v3i32` call exactly like a scalar one. The fix belongs in `SIMDize.cpp` itself, not an earlier pass: `checkVectorDecompositionSupported` didn't recognize a matched `feme.cpu.masked.store.*` call's stored-value operand as a supported consumer of a decomposed divergent vector (only a matched `feme.cpu.resource.*` store's was), and `FunctionWidener::widenMaskedStore` unconditionally called the scalar-only `getWidened` on its stored value, which (for a vector-typed, already-decomposed operand recorded only in `WidenedVectorComponents`, not `Widened`) fell through to `getWidened`'s generic broadcast fallback and tried to build an illegal `<W x <3 x i32>>` vector-of-vectors. Fixed both: `checkVectorDecompositionSupported` now accepts a matched `feme.cpu.masked.store.*` call's stored-value operand the same way it already accepts a matched resource-store call's, and `widenMaskedStore` now recognizes a vector-typed stored value, reassembles each lane's own `<3 x i32>` from the decomposed per-component wide values (mirroring `widenResourceCall`'s equivalent per-lane reassembly), and writes it individually per lane with a load-select-store idiom (mirroring `MeshOutputWrapper.cpp`'s `lowerMeshOutputStore`) instead of `llvm.masked.scatter`, which has no vector-of-vector-element form to lower a per-lane vector value to. New lit regression `feme/test/Transforms/CPU/simdize-masked-memop-vector-divergent.ll` and unit test `SIMDizeTest.DecomposesInsertElementChainIntoMaskedVectorStore`. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build): 1955/2014 passing (59 pre-existing `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`, up from 1953/2012 by exactly the 2 new tests). A real re-run of the exact 218-case `vkRefUtil.cpp:37` bucket (`bucket_refutil37_h6g_b_a_i_a.txt`, unchanged) with a combined stdout/stderr diagnostic log confirms the fix directly: this row's own 148 `feme-cpu-simdize` divergent-vector rejections drop to **80** (68 cases -- exactly the `gl_PrimitiveTriangleIndicesEXT` shape this row named -- progress further, landing in the already-tracked `feme-cpu-wrap-mesh-output` bucket, which grows from 15 to 83 cases as a direct, expected consequence), with every other bucket (`feme-cpu-wrap-fragment` 2, `feme-cpu-linearize` 13, `spirv.MemoryBarrier` 11, `spirv.AtomicExchange` 4, `spirv.EXT.EmitMeshTasks` 1, specialization constants 7, rasterizer/depth state 7, `Symbols not found` 5 (H6g-b-c), the lone sampled-image/sampler `UnsupportedOps` remainder, and a 3-case color-blend-state mismatch) unaffected. The remaining 80 `feme-cpu-simdize` cases surface a new dominant blocker in their place -- a divergent vector value used as a vector comparison (`fcmp`/`icmp`) operand, an entirely different consumer shape from this row's own masked-store fix -- tracked as new row H6g-b-a-i-a-i-b below. A full `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.*` re-run (28044 cases, resume-loop methodology) additionally confirms this row's fix, as an unplanned but verified side effect, also closes H6g-b-b: that row's crash (`FunctionWidener::widenMaskedStore` -> `getWidened` -> `ConstantVector::getSplat`'s `isValidElementType` assertion) is the exact same code path this row's fix touches, and its "aggregate" description was an imprecise characterization of the same vector-of-vector-element rejection all along, not a distinct genuine-aggregate (struct/array) case -- the re-run's 14 previously-blacklisted crashing cases (`barrier_in_task`, `group_memory_barrier_in_task_*`, `memory_barrier_shared_in_task_*`, `emit_in_control_flow*`, `payload_not_accessed`) all now complete cleanly with **0** crashes (down from 14, requiring 0 resume-loop iterations instead of the prior 15), each landing in the same `vkRefUtil.cpp:37` bucket (232 cases now, up from 218 by exactly these 14) with an ordinary, non-crashing diagnostic instead (confirmed individually for `payload_not_accessed`: now `feme-cpu-wrap-mesh-output`, no crash) -- see H6g-b-b's own updated row. The 1957-case `draw_sample.txt` regression sample stays byte-identical to every prior row (14 passed / 153 failed / 1790 not supported), **0 regressions**. `FeMeCPUDesign.md`/`Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed no change needed: this is a completeness fix within `SIMDizePass`'s existing "vectors become components" decomposition scope (documented in `feme/docs/FeMeCPUDesign.md`'s "Phase 4: Widening"), not a new feature or extension surface. Milestone H6 does **not** close: this row's own fix and H6g-b-b's incidental closure both land, but the same re-run surfaces H6g-b-a-i-a-i-b as a new dominant blocker, alongside the already-tracked H6g-b-c. | H6g-b-a-i-a-i-b | ~~**A divergent vector value used as a vector comparison (`fcmp`/`icmp`) operand is rejected by `feme-cpu-simdize` in the same `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`/`vkRefUtil.cpp:37` bucket ("function 'main' has a divergent vector value ... used outside a supported insertelement-chain/resource-store/extractelement/select/shufflevector/phi/elementwise pattern; component decomposition is not yet supported for this use")**, now the new dominant first-emitted FeMe/MLIR diagnostic in that bucket once H6g-b-a-i-a-i-a's own masked-store fix lets those shaders progress further (80 of 218 cases in a combined stdout/stderr diagnostic rerun land here, e.g. `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.in_out.32_bits_only.permutation_0.mesh_only`'s `%8 = insertelement <4 x float> %6, float %7, i64 3` used by `%16 = fcmp ole <4 x float> %8, %15`, confirmed via the same one-off diagnostic-dump-and-single-case-rerun technique H6g-b-a-i-a-i-a used). Root cause not yet isolated: a vector comparison producing a `<N x i1>` result is not among `checkVectorDecompositionSupported`'s accepted consumer shapes at all today (unlike a matched resource-store's or masked-store's stored-value operand), and the design's own existing "a `select` with a per-lane `<N x i1>` condition remains diagnosed" deviation (`checkVectorDecompositionSupported`'s file comment) suggests decomposing a per-lane vector *condition* end-to-end -- not just a per-lane vector *value* -- may need broader work than a single consumer-acceptance addition; needs a real failing shader/IR reduction to confirm the exact GLSL/SPIR-V source shape (a component-wise `lessThanEqual`/`greaterThan`-style comparison feeding a `select`, most likely) and whether the right fix is a narrow `fcmp`/`icmp` consumer-and-producer addition mirroring this row's own sibling, or the broader per-lane-condition decomposition the file comment already anticipates~~ (done: added `fcmp`/`icmp` (`CmpInst`) as a supported vector producer (its `<N x i1>` result decomposes into `N` per-component `<W x i1>` comparisons exactly like ordinary elementwise arithmetic) and consumer, and generalized the design's own documented `select`-condition deviation away entirely: a `select`'s condition may now be a per-lane `<N x i1>` vector (decomposed into `N` widened `<W x i1>` components, one used per per-component `select`, in `widenVectorSelect`), not just a shared scalar `i1` (`dc7fe7b15f2f`, lit tests `simdize-vector-fcmp-select.ll`/updated `simdize-vector-select.ll`/`simdize-vector-unsupported.ll`, unit test `SIMDizeTest.DecomposesVectorComparisonIntoPerLaneSelectCondition` in `bf6a561b0531`). Running the *real* VK-GL-CTS `deqp-vk` (not a simulated count) against this row's own named `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.in_out.32_bits_only.permutation_0.mesh_only` case with `FEME_VULKAN_LOG_CREATION_ERRORS=1` confirmed the fix was necessary but not sufficient: the same one-off diagnostic-dump-and-single-case-rerun technique found the real rejected shape was not a `select` at all -- glslang lowers GLSL's `all(lessThanEqual(a, b))` to `llvm.vector.reduce.and.v4i1(fcmp ole <4 x float> %a, %b)`, an `llvm.vector.reduce.*` intrinsic call folding a divergent vector's components together, a shape not yet accepted as a consumer. Added `isSupportedVectorReduceIntrinsic` (accepting the integer/comparison reduces: `and`/`or`/`xor`/`add`/`mul`/`smax`/`smin`/`umax`/`umin`; the floating-point reduces are excluded as out of scope/unneeded for now) and `widenVectorReduce`, folding a vector operand's `N` components together two at a time with the matching op, landing its (non-vector-typed) result in the ordinary `Widened` map. Re-running the same real case again still failed, with a third, new blocker: a GLSL `min()`/`max()`/`clamp()` builtin lowers to `llvm.minnum`/`llvm.maxnum`/`llvm.smin`/`llvm.smax` intrinsic calls over an already-decomposed divergent vector operand, another shape not yet accepted as either a producer or a consumer. Generalized `checkVectorDecompositionSupported`, `widenVectorElementwise` (now also handling `CallInst`, using `I.args()` instead of `I.operands()` since a call's own operand list also includes its callee), and `widenInstruction`'s dispatch to accept any vector-typed, homogeneous "trivially vectorizable" intrinsic call (`isElementwiseVectorizableIntrinsic`) as both a producer and a consumer, decomposing it exactly like ordinary elementwise arithmetic: one scalar-element intrinsic call per component (`eaf8216ce1f4`, lit tests `simdize-vector-reduce.ll`/`simdize-vector-intrinsic.ll`, updated `simdize-vector-unsupported.ll`; unit tests `SIMDizeTest.DecomposesVectorComparisonIntoReduceAnd`/`DecomposesHomogeneousVectorizableIntrinsicCall` in `08c1e2b9d4e5`/`3cb7e072fda2`). With all three fixes, re-running the same single named case now gets past `feme-cpu-simdize` entirely -- it fails only at `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` time on an unrelated, separate JIT-symbol-registration gap (missing `feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v2f32`/`v3f32`/`v2i32`/`v3i32` overloads in `feme/runtime/CPU/FeMeRuntimeCPU.c`, which only implements the scalar and `v4f32`/`v4i32` raw-load/store overloads today), a different subsystem entirely (JIT runtime symbol registration, not `SIMDizePass` decomposition), out of scope for this row and tracked as new row H6g-b-a-i-a-i-c below. Re-running the full real `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.in_out.*` bucket (560 cases, the row's own cited 80/218-case bucket, via `--deqp-caselist-file`) confirms this at scale: still 0 passed / **80** failed / 480 not-supported (unchanged totals), but every one of the 80 failures now hits that same new JIT-symbol blocker instead of the `feme-cpu-simdize` diagnostic this row targets (spot-checked across several cases in the bucket, not just the one named case), confirming this row's own `SIMDizePass`-level fix is complete and this bucket's dominant blocker has fully moved to H6g-b-a-i-a-i-c. `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build): 1961/2020 passing (59 pre-existing `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`, up from 1955/2014 by exactly the 6 new tests across all three fixes). `FeMeCPUDesign.md` updated (Phase 4's decomposition deviation bullet now documents nine producer shapes, was six; `13051214ea77`) to reflect all three additions and retire the now-resolved per-lane-condition-`select` deviation note. `Vulkan14FeatureInventory.md`/`VulkanExtensionInventory.md` confirmed no change needed: a `SIMDizePass`-completeness fix within its existing "vectors become components" decomposition scope, not a new feature or extension surface. Milestone H6 does **not** close: this row's fix lands, but the same re-run surfaces H6g-b-a-i-a-i-c as the bucket's new dominant blocker, alongside the already-tracked H6g-b-c. | H6g-b-a-i-a-i-c | ~~**`vkCreateGraphicsPipelines` fails at JIT-link time with "Symbols not found: [ feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v2f32, feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v3f32, feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v3i32, feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v2i32, feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v4i32 ]"**, now the new dominant blocker in the same 80-case `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.in_out.*` bucket H6g-b-a-i-a-i-b's own `fcmp`/`icmp`/reduce/vectorizable-intrinsic fixes let those cases progress past `feme-cpu-simdize` entirely (confirmed by re-running the full 560-case bucket and spot-checking several individual cases with `FEME_VULKAN_LOG_CREATION_ERRORS=1` against the real `deqp-vk`/`feme` Vulkan ICD once H6g-b-a-i-a-i-b's own fix landed). Root cause isolated: `feme/runtime/CPU/FeMeRuntimeCPU.c` only defines the scalar (`feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.i32`/`.f32`) and full-`<4 x T>`-width (`feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v4f32`/`.v4i32`) raw-buffer-load overloads today (see its own `asm("feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v4f32")`-labeled function and neighbors) -- the 2- and 3-component overloads a `vec2`/`vec3`/`ivec2`/`ivec3` mesh-shader input/output actually needs are simply missing, so the JIT's `orc::LLJIT` can't materialize a call to them at pipeline-creation time. The store-side (`feme.cpu.resource.store.raw.*`) and typed-buffer (`feme.cpu.resource.{load,store}.typed.v4{f32,i32}`) paths were not checked for the same v2/v3 gap and may need the same fix. Not yet fixed: needs new `feme.cpu.resource.load.raw.v2f32`/`.v3f32`/`.v2i32`/`.v3i32` (and likely the store-side/typed-buffer counterparts, pending the same gap check) runtime functions mirroring the existing `v4f32`/`v4i32` ones' bindless-descriptor-lookup-then-masked-load shape, plus confirming `feme::cpu::ResourceCalls`/`ResourceLowering.cpp` already emit calls to these names for narrower vector widths (or need their own fix to do so)~~ (done: added the 5 missing raw-buffer-load overloads (`v2f32`/`v3f32`/`v2i32`/`v3i32`/`v4i32`) to `feme/runtime/CPU/FeMeRuntimeCPU.c`, plus their symmetric store-side counterparts proactively (this row's own "may need the same fix" note) -- `feme::cpu::ResourceCalls`/`ResourceLowering.cpp` already emitted calls to these mangled names for every vector width with no changes needed, since `mangleResourceCallName` is fully generic over element type/width. Found and fixed a real, related bug while adding the v3-wide stores: Clang's C ABI coerces a by-value `<3 x float>`/`<3 x i32>` parameter to a `<4 x i32>` register pair and pads `sizeof()` to 16, so a naive `*(Unaligned *)Ptr = Value` or even `__builtin_memcpy(Ptr, &Value, sizeof(Value))` store body emitted a widened, out-of-bounds 16-byte `store <4 x T>` instead of the intended 12-byte one; fixed with `__builtin_memcpy(Ptr, &Value, 12)` using an explicit literal byte count. Verified the production JIT-link path (declare-then-`Linker::linkInModule`) is unaffected by the coercion mismatch itself (confirmed via a temporary probe test mirroring the real caller/linker order, since RAUW-based linking never re-validates a `CallInst`'s `FunctionType` against its callee). Added 5 new `RawLoadStoreRoundTrip*` unit tests to `RuntimeCPUTest.cpp` (generalizing its `addStoreWrapper` helper to detect and adapt to an ABI-coerced target parameter type) and 10 new `CHECK-DAG` lines to `runtime-cpu-bitcode.test`; `ninja check-feme` rises from 1961/2020 to 1966/2025 (5 new passing tests), 0 regressions. A real `deqp-vk`/`feme` Vulkan ICD re-run of this row's own named case and the full 560-case `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.in_out.*` bucket confirms the exact reported JIT symbol-not-found error is gone from all 80 previously-blocked cases; see `VulkanCTSReport.md`'s own "Roadmap H6g-b-a-i-a-i-c" section for the two new blockers this exposes (tracked as H6g-b-c, already-known, and the new H6g-b-d)) | H6g-b-a-i-a-i-b | `feme/runtime/CPU/FeMeRuntimeCPU.c` | P2 |
| H6g-b-b | ~~**`feme::cpu::SIMDizePass`'s `FunctionWidener::widenMaskedStore` -> `getWidened` asserts (`ConstantVector::getSplat`/`llvm::FixedVectorType::get`'s own `isValidElementType` check) when a masked store's stored value has an aggregate (non-scalar, non-pointer) element type**, newly reachable now that H6g-b's own fix lets real mesh/task content compile far enough to reach `SIMDizePass` at all -- originally 3 `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.misc.*` cases (`emit_in_control_flow`, `emit_in_control_flow_bad_emit_last`, `payload_not_accessed`), now **14** cases (H6g-b-a's own fix lets 11 further cases -- `barrier_in_task`, `group_memory_barrier_in_task_{array,float,struct,uint64,vector}`, `memory_barrier_shared_in_task_{array,float,struct,uint64,vector}` -- progress far enough through deserialization and legalization to reach this same crash too, confirmed via `gdb` backtrace on `barrier_in_task`: identical stack to the original 3) abort the whole `deqp-vk` process with this assertion (confirmed via `gdb` backtrace: `SIMDizePass::run` -> `FunctionWidener::widenInstruction` -> `widenMaskedStore` -> `getWidened` -> `ConstantVector::getSplat`), rather than failing cleanly. `getWidened`'s constant-splat and non-constant (`CreateVectorSplat`) paths were both written assuming a per-lane scalar/pointer value, mirroring every other masked-memory-op operand this file widens; a task-payload-store's payload value (or some other operand reaching this path with an aggregate LLVM type) breaks that assumption, since LLVM vectors cannot hold an aggregate element type at all. | H6g-b-c | ~~**A mesh entry's unresolved arrayed-builtin-block access -- left unrewritten by `H6c-a-a-iii`'s own `resolveOffsetWithinElement` fix (`std::nullopt`, "leave for `ValidateStagePass` to diagnose") -- is never actually diagnosed, because `ValidateStagePass::run` still does not validate `ShaderStage::Mesh` at all (every prior row that touched mesh validation, from `H6a` on, left this unreachable/not-yet-wired)**, so the raw, un-canonicalized global-variable access survives all the way to `feme::cpu`'s JIT, which then fails with a genuinely undefined symbol (confirmed directly: `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.builtin.cull_primitives`, one of this row's own 33 `vkPipelineConstructionUtil.cpp:176` cases, fails with `JIT session error: Symbols not found: [ spirv_var_16 ]`) instead of a clean, diagnosable compile-time rejection -- the exact same 9-case-turned-33-case set `H6c-a-a-ii`/`H6c-a-a-iii`'s own reports already named (`cull_primitives`, `draw_index_in_{mesh,task}`, `local_invocation_{id,index}_in_task`, `position`, `primitive_id_glsl`, `work_group_id_in_{mesh,task}`). This is the concrete, now-reachable instance of the gap `H6c-a-a-iii`'s own report already flagged as a future risk ("not yet reachable... mirroring `TaskPayloadStore`'s own 'not yet reachable' precedent")~~ (done: `ValidateStagePass::run` now validates `ShaderStage::Mesh` alongside `Vertex`/`Fragment`, which in turn required extending `isStageOpLegalForStage`'s `InputLoad`/`OutputStore` case to also accept `Mesh` (a mesh entry's own per-vertex/per-primitive output write reuses `OutputStore`, roadmap H6b -- without this, every legitimate mesh output store would have been newly, wrongly rejected as "not legal" the moment `Mesh` was wired in). Added a new check, `validateStageIOGlobalAccess`, walking every load/store's pointer operand back through any `getelementptr` chain (`GEPOperator`) to find its underlying `GlobalVariable`: one `isSPIRVStageIOGlobal` still recognizes (address space 7/8, carrying `!spirv.Decorations`/`!feme.spirv.MemberDecorations`) means `CanonicalizeStagePass` left that access un-rewritten, so it is now diagnosed directly instead of silently reaching the JIT. `isSPIRVStageIOGlobal` itself was factored out of `CanonicalizeStage.cpp`'s anonymous namespace into a new shared header, `feme/include/feme/Transforms/Graphics/StageIOGlobal.h`, so both passes recognize the identical shape (no behavior change to `CanonicalizeStagePass`). New unit tests in `ValidateStageTest.cpp`: `MeshOutputStoreIsLegal`/`DiscardInMeshStageIsIllegal` (confirming the newly-wired stage's own legality rules), `UnresolvedStageIOGlobalAccessInMeshIsDiagnosed` (reusing `CanonicalizeStageTest.ConstantIndexIntoArrayedBuiltinInterfaceBlockIsLeftUnrewritten`'s own arrayed-`PerPrimitiveEXT`-block IR directly against `ValidateStagePass`, confirming the exact shape this row targets is caught), and `OrdinaryAllocaAccessInMeshIsNotDiagnosed` (a negative control, confirming an unrelated local `alloca` access is not flagged). `ninja check-feme` (assertions-enabled, ccache build): 1972/2031 (59 pre-existing, unrelated `Unsupported`, 0 `Failed`), up from H6k's own 1968/2027 baseline by exactly the 4 new tests this row adds. A real `deqp-vk` re-run of this row's own named case confirms the fix directly: `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.builtin.cull_primitives` no longer reaches the JIT at all -- `feme-graphics-validate-stage: ... has an unresolved stage-IO global-variable access to 'spirv_var_16', a shape CanonicalizeStagePass does not yet canonicalize into a 'feme.stage.*' call` is now the first diagnostic emitted, well before pipeline creation fails (still `Fail`, `VK_ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED` at `vkPipelineConstructionUtil.cpp:176` -- diagnosed, not fixed, exactly this row's own scope). A full re-run of the real `dEQP-VK.mesh_shader.ext.builtin.*` group (37 cases) confirms this at scale: of the 9-case-turned-33-case set's mesh-portion cases, 12 now hit this row's own new clean diagnostic instead of a JIT symbol error (`cull_primitives`, `draw_index_in_{mesh,task}`, `local_invocation_{id,index}_in_task`, `num_work_groups_mesh`, `num_work_groups_task_and_mesh`, `position`, `primitive_id_{glsl,spirv}`, `work_group_id_in_{mesh,task}`); the other 10 failing cases in the group hit an unrelated, pre-existing `feme-cpu-simdize` divergent-vector-value diagnostic further down the pipeline, confirmed unaffected by this row (0 `feme-graphics-validate-stage` diagnostics for any of them); group totals stay 0 Pass / 22 Fail / 15 NotSupported, unchanged from before this row (expected -- diagnosing a failure earlier and more cleanly does not itself turn it into a pass).
